# Portfolio — Consolidated Research Synthesis

_Compiled 2026-05-31. Informational synthesis, not personalized financial advice._

Pulling together independent analyses (own deep research + two uploaded reports). All three converge on the same diagnosis; divergences are mostly dates and a few figures flagged at the end.

---

## What this portfolio actually is

A deliberately constructed, concentrated **"quality compounder" book** built on four structural bets plus a defensive compliance sleeve. The businesses are genuinely high-quality with real moats and proven capital allocation — the issues are **thematic concentration and correlated valuation risk, not business quality**.

| Cluster | Weight | Holdings |
|---------|--------|----------|
| Vertical-market software / serial acquirers | 36% | Constellation 12, ATOSS 9, Topicus 9, Vitec 6 |
| Life-sciences supply chain | 24% | Bachem 9, Sartorius Stedim 6, Eckert & Ziegler 5, Bioventix 4 |
| Family-controlled holding companies | 18% | EXOR 8, Investor AB 7, Sofina 3 |
| Defensive "sovereignty / compliance" | 12% | Veralto 8, Secunet 4 |
| Emerging Asia | 10% | iShares India 6, VOF 4 |

> Veralto is the one classification judgment call — loosely folded into life sciences in one report, but sits more cleanly as a defensive regulated-compliance compounder alongside Secunet. Either way it's a low-correlation stabilizer.

- Top 5 names ≈ **47%** of capital; top 10 ≈ **80%** — meaningful concentration.
- Founder/family alignment runs through almost every holding.

---

## The central tension: fundamentals strong, prices weak

The defining feature in 2026. Businesses kept compounding through 2025 while share prices were crushed by the market's "AI-will-kill-software" de-rating and the bioprocessing hangover.

**The fundamentals:**
- Constellation — FY2025 revenue +15% to US$11.6bn; FCF to shareholders +14% to US$1.68bn
- Topicus — +20% for the year, +23% in Q1 2026
- ATOSS — +11%, 36% EBIT margin, cloud now ~49% of revenue
- Vitec — +9%, 88% recurring revenue
- Veralto — adjusted EPS +10% to $3.90
- Secunet — revenue +13%, order intake/backlog up ~26–36%
- Sartorius — +9.6% constant-currency, margins expanding
- Bachem — ~+19% in local currency

**The prices, against that backdrop:**

| Name | Roughly | From its high | Multiple |
|------|---------|---------------|----------|
| Constellation | CAD ~2,684 | ~−47% (high 5,060) | ~58x TTM |
| ATOSS | €~72 | ~−51% (high 147.6) | ~24x (vs ~48 median) |
| Sartorius Stedim | €~163–178 | ~−65–70% vs 2021 ATH | ~58x TTM |
| Bioventix | £~17 | ~−44% | ~6% yield |
| Secunet | €~202 | ~−18% | — |
| EXOR | €~60–65 | trades ~50%+ below NAV | — |

That gap is the whole investment question: if the moats survive (the consensus view), this is an **opportunity set, not a thesis break**.

---

## Cluster-by-cluster

### Vertical-market software (36%) — the engine and the key debate
Constellation, Topicus, Vitec, ATOSS monetize mission-critical niche software with high switching costs and recycle cash into fragmented M&A (ATOSS being the organic exception). All three reports agree on the AI question: horizontal software is exposed, but **vertical, regulation-embedded systems of record** retain wide moats, and cheaper private-software valuations actually widen the acquisition pipeline. Tripwire: organic growth and churn, not headlines. **Structural note:** Constellation controls Topicus, so the ~21% in those two is effectively one capital-allocation ecosystem; correlated VMS exposure is really the full 36%.

### Life-sciences supply chain (24%) — picks-and-shovels, one shared cycle
Supplier/manufacturing positions rather than clinical risk. Bachem (peptide/oligo CDMO) and Sartorius (single-use bioprocessing, ~85% recurring consumables) share GLP-1/biologics exposure and one bioprocessing investment cycle — a GLP-1 air-pocket or overcapacity hits ~15% of the book together. Eckert & Ziegler (radiopharma isotopes, riding the radioligand-therapy wave toward a ~$26bn-by-2030 market) and Bioventix (diagnostic antibody royalties) are more idiosyncratic.

### Holding companies (18%) — discounted optionality with a trap risk
- **EXOR** (Agnelli) — ~50%+ discount to NAV near 52-week lows, buying back stock, rotating out of autos into healthcare (Philips, bioMérieux), ~€3.5–3.7bn dry powder from Iveco-related disposals; Lingotto (>$10bn AUM) returned ~40% in 2025.
- **Investor AB** (Wallenberg) — highest-quality, lowest-risk holdco; diversified blue-chips plus Patricia Industries, NAV ~SEK 355/share end-2025 (~SEK 367 by Q1 2026), tiny leverage.
- **Sofina** — most venture-exposed (≈45% private funds), recovering from prior write-downs, now net-cash, acting as a liquidity provider.
- **Hidden overlap:** all three hold European healthcare/industrial and private-growth assets; Investor AB's EQT stake plus Sofina duplicate private-market exposure.

### Defensive sleeve (12%)
- **Veralto** — water + product quality, Danaher-style, >60% recurring, ~23% margins, water-scarcity and packaging tailwinds; arguably the cleanest all-weather compounder in the book.
- **Secunet** — Germany's high-assurance cyber/defence partner; certification barriers lock out foreign rivals; Defence & Space now ~⅓ of revenue and growing fast; fortress balance sheet (negative Q1 2026 EBIT was front-loaded hiring/seasonality).

### Emerging Asia (10%)
- **India (ETF)** — liquid demographic/credit-growth bet; lagging, persistent foreign outflows, large-caps now below historical valuation.
- **Vietnam (VOF)** — frontier supply-chain-relocation bet; ~8% GDP growth, cheap (~10x forward), ~20%+ discount to NAV, but illiquid and higher-governance-risk.

---

## Historical performance (treat as optimistic model output)

Perplexity-style report estimates portfolio CAGR ~19.2%, volatility ~12.1%, return/volatility ratio ~1.59 over 8–10 years. Standout individual CAGRs: Vitec ~32%, Constellation ~29%, Eckert & Ziegler ~28%, Secunet ~27%, ATOSS ~25%; several names up 500–1,700% over a decade.

**Two caveats:**
1. The 12% volatility assumes low correlation, which is unrealistic — software names move together, holdcos move together — so realised volatility and drawdowns will be higher (that report concedes 30–40% drawdowns are plausible).
2. The "risk-adjusted" ratios are CAGR-over-volatility, not true Sharpe ratios. Directionally useful; precisely, no.

---

## Consolidated risk picture

- **AI re-rating of software moats** — single biggest swing factor, hits the largest cluster directly.
- **Quality-compounder valuation unwind** — premium multiples would de-rate together in a style rotation.
- **Correlation clustering** — software-together and healthcare-together means realised vol exceeds the model.
- **CSU/TOI duplication** — ~21% in one controlled ecosystem.
- **Single bioprocessing/GLP-1 cycle** — Bachem + Sartorius linked; ~15% moves as one.
- **Holdco discount-trap** — especially EXOR; family control limits pressure to close the gap.
- **EM macro/FX/political** — India outflows and rupee; Vietnam frontier risk; USD/INR weakness alone cut Sofina's 2025 NAV by ~€925m.
- **Idiosyncratic** — Bioventix's June 2032 Siemens troponin cliff plus China substitution; Eckert & Ziegler's partner-dependence, lumpy licence income, 2025 cyberattack.

---

## Strengths and gaps

**Strengths:** durable moats, recurring revenue, high returns on capital, disciplined capital allocators, owner alignment, and a de-rating that has handed historically cheap entry points on several names.

**Gap:** the book is stylistically monolithic — almost everything is a European-tilted quality compounder. Light on US large-cap breadth, mega-cap technology (ironically, given the AI risk it carries), energy/commodities, real assets, and direct financials. Currency is diversified but EUR-heavy.

---

## What all three reports independently recommend
_(Scenarios, not advice.)_

1. **Treat the VMS de-rating as the central decision.** If vertical moats survive AI (the consensus), weakness in Constellation/Topicus/ATOSS/Vitec is an opportunity. Reassess only if organic growth falls below ~3% for several quarters or net retention deteriorates.
2. **Decide whether the ~21% CSU+TOI overlap reflects intended conviction;** if not, diversifying one toward the other (or a non-software compounder) reduces single-ecosystem risk.
3. **Cap the linked Bachem+Sartorius cycle;** watch capacity-utilization and GLP-1 volume guidance — first place to trim if overcapacity signals build.
4. **Use the holdco discounts deliberately, watching cash deployment:** successful EXOR deployment of ~€3.5bn+ at a wide discount is NAV-accretive; failure widens the discount.
5. **Hold Bioventix for cash flow but track the Alzheimer's/Tau pivot** against the 2032 troponin cliff — that pivot reaching routine clinical use is make-or-break.
6. **Consider genuinely uncorrelated exposure** (energy/real assets, US/mega-cap, fixed income) if this is the whole portfolio.
7. **Hold the EM sleeve at a size you can stomach** through volatility; a 6–12 month re-rating possibility on a decade-long horizon.

---

## Where the reports disagree — numbers to verify before acting

- **Bachem 2026 outlook** — second upload describes 35–45% local-currency growth, >CHF 1bn sales. That growth rate is at the extreme end for a CDMO; treat as unverified — the >CHF 1bn figure is more plausibly a medium-term ambition than a 2026 target. Confirm against Bachem's actual FY2025 report.
- **Sartorius** — did **not** issue a 2026 profit warning (some low-quality aggregators claim it did). Guidance confirmed at Q1 2026; early-year dip was a miss-vs-consensus, not a cut.
- **Eckert & Ziegler net-income growth** — shows as +46% in one report, +22% in another; that report's segment revenues don't sum cleanly to the €312m total. Solid anchors: ~€312m sales and €0.78 EPS; treat growth rate loosely.
- **Investor AB NAV (SEK 355 vs 367)** and **ATOSS cash (€162m vs €115m)** — differ only by reporting date (end-2025 vs Q1 2026), not real disagreements.
- **Bioventix Tau revenue** — appears as ~£605k and ~£150k; different periods (FY2025 full-year vs H1 FY2026 royalties). Small but growing fast.

---

_This is informational synthesis, not personalized financial advice. Position sizing, tax and currency hedging depend on individual circumstances._

## Possible next steps
- One-page reconciled dashboard (weights, price-vs-high, key metric, main risk, monitoring tripwire per holding).
- Deep-dive on the one decision that moves the needle: whether the VMS moats hold against AI.

# Portfolio — The Honest Read (Qualitative Assessment)

_Compiled 2026-05-31. Informational synthesis, not personalized financial advice._

## The core thesis

This is one of the more coherent and genuinely well-built portfolios around — and that coherence is **simultaneously its greatest strength and its single biggest vulnerability**. The distinction is the whole thing.

Most portfolios are accidents — a pile of names accumulated with no unifying idea. This is **a thesis expressed as a portfolio**. Almost every holding is the same kind of thing: a cash-generative business with high switching costs, run by an owner-operator or family whose incentives are aligned with patient, long-term value creation rather than next quarter. Constellation's acquisition flywheel, ATOSS's compliance-embedded software, Bachem's regulatory lock-in, EXOR's permanent capital, Veralto's razor-and-blade water franchise — these rhyme. That's a worldview, and a good one. Over a decade, owning durable compounders bought by disciplined allocators is about as sound a philosophy as exists in public markets.

## The catch: diversified-looking, not diversified

The same quality that makes it elegant makes it **fragile in one specific way: it isn't actually diversified, it just looks diversified**. Fifteen names across software, healthcare, holding companies, and emerging Asia feels like breadth, but underneath it's essentially **one bet** — that high-quality compounding works and the market eventually pays for it.

- When that style is in favor, everything rises together and you look like a genius.
- When it's out of favor (as recently), everything sits underwater together — which is what's happening now.
- **There is no genuine ballast:** no energy or real assets, no mega-cap tech, very little outside Europe, no cash or bonds to redeploy when things get cheap.

Built to win big in the world where quality compounding keeps working; **no hedge** for the world where that style stays unloved for five years.

## The AI question — the real fork in the road

Genuinely unresolved. The lean: the market is wrong to treat vertical, mission-critical, regulation-embedded software the way it treats generic horizontal software — the workflows Constellation, ATOSS and Topicus sell are precisely what AI **can't** cheaply rip out. But that's a judgment, not a fact, and the market's fear isn't stupid. The de-rating could be a gift, or the first innings of a real structural impairment that takes years to become obvious. **Nobody knows yet.**

Worth naming clearly:
- ~**36%** of the portfolio rides on the optimistic answer to an open question.
- ~**21%** sits in a single controlled ecosystem (Constellation runs Topicus).
- That's a high-conviction position — defensible **if deliberate and sized on purpose**. The honest thing to interrogate: is the concentration *chosen*, or did it *accrete* because you admire these businesses?

## De-rated is not the same as cheap

Cuts against the easy "everything's on sale, back up the truck" framing. Several names — Constellation and Sartorius both around **58x earnings** — have fallen a long way and are still **not cheap in absolute terms**. Part of what's happening is air coming out of genuinely stretched 2021 multiples, not a clean mispricing.

The clearest actual value is **narrower** than the broad weakness suggests:
- Holding-company discounts (**EXOR at a ~50% gap to NAV** is the standout).
- Arguably **ATOSS** after its collapse.
- With the highest-quality names like Veralto, you're paying up for quality and getting a defensive compounder — fine, but don't confuse it with bargain-hunting.

## The single most useful thought: the biggest risk is behavioral

The biggest risk to the outcome probably isn't any business in here — **it's behavioral**. This portfolio only delivers for someone who can:
- Hold through a multi-year stretch of looking wrong.
- Tune out the AI-doom narrative without becoming dogmatically dismissive of it.
- Keep **re-underwriting the businesses** rather than reacting to the prices.

The portfolio is already stress-testing exactly that temperament, with nearly everything below its highs. The real damage in a book like this almost never comes from the companies failing — it comes from **the holder capitulating at the bottom of a scare that later proves overblown**.

So the real question isn't "are these good businesses" (they mostly are) — it's: **"Do I have the stomach and the time horizon this strategy demands, and am I comfortable having no airbag if quality stays out of favor longer than I expect?"**

---

_Not a prediction or advice; not a financial advisor. The one thread most worth pulling: the AI-versus-vertical-software question, since ~36% of the outcome hinges on it — concretely distinguishing "the moats are holding" from "the moats are eroding" so you have real tripwires rather than vibes._

# Disputed Figures — Verification

_Verification date: 2026-05-31. Primary sources: company FY2025 / Q1 2026 reports and IR pages, plus reputable press (RTT News, Investing.com, Maynard Paton, Stockopedia)._

## Summary table

| # | Claim | Verdict | Primary source |
|---|-------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | Bachem FY2026 guidance 35–45% LC growth | **CONFIRMED** | Bachem Media Release FY2025 / RTT News |
| 1 | Bachem ">CHF 1bn" is a 2026 target, not just medium-term | **PARTIALLY CONFIRMED** (skeptic partly wrong: it IS a 2026 ambition) | ad-hoc-news |
| 1 | Bachem ~+19% LC growth in 2025 | **CONFIRMED** (19.2% LC; 14.8% reported) | Bachem FY2025 PDF |
| 2 | Sartorius issued a 2026 profit warning | **REFUTED** — guidance confirmed, no warning | Sartorius Q1 2026 corporate news |
| 2 | Sartorius ~+9.6% constant-currency growth | **REFUTED / mislabeled** — Q1 2026 group growth was +7.5% cc | Same as above |
| 3 | E&Z net-income growth +46% (vs +22%) | **CONFIRMED at +46%** (net income €48.8m vs €33.3m) | EQS-News FY2025 |
| 3 | E&Z ~€312m sales, €0.78 EPS | **CONFIRMED** | Same |
| 3 | E&Z segment revenues sum to total | **REFUTED** — segments (€321.4m) exceed group total (€312.0m) | Same |
| 4 | Bioventix Tau ~£605k (FY2025 FY) vs ~£150k (H1 FY2026 royalties) | **CONFIRMED** — different periods/metrics | Bioventix FY2025 + H1 FY2026 |
| 4 | Bioventix ~£17 price, ~−44% from high | **CONFIRMED** (1,675p; −44.5% from ~3,019p high) | Stockopedia |
| 4 | Bioventix ~6% dividend yield | **REFUTED (now)** — currently ~8.8–9.0%; the 6% figure was true earlier at the ~£24 price | Same |

---

## 1. BACHEM (SIX: BANB)

**FY2025 actuals:** Net sales CHF 695.1m (CHF 605.3m prior year) = +14.8% reported, **+19.2% in local currencies**. EBITDA CHF 214.7m (margin 30.9%). Net profit CHF 148.8m (+24%).

**FY2026 guidance:** "Full-year 2026 sales growth of **35% to 45% in local currencies**, with EBITDA margin in the low-30% range." The 35–45% figure in the second report is accurate, not an error.

**The ">CHF 1bn" question:** The skeptic claimed this is a medium-term ambition, not a 2026 target — this is **partly wrong**. Reputable reporting states Bachem "aims for annual sales of more than CHF 1bn and EBITDA margin over 30% **in 2026**." Mechanically consistent: CHF 695m × 1.45 ≈ CHF 1.0bn, so >CHF 1bn is the top end of the same 35–45% 2026 guidance, driven by the Building K ramp-up (first phase Swissmedic-inspected end-2025). It is an aspirational 2026 figure contingent on the upper end of guidance — not a separate, later "medium-term" target. The skeptic's broader point that 35–45% is extreme for a CDMO is valid context: press flagged single-facility (Building K) execution risk in this target.

**Verdict:** Guidance 35–45% and ~+19% LC growth **CONFIRMED**. ">CHF 1bn as a medium-term-only ambition" **PARTIALLY CONFIRMED** — it is presented as a 2026 ambition (top of guidance).

## 2. SARTORIUS (Sartorius AG / Sartorius Stedim Biotech)

**No profit warning was issued.** Q1 2026 (reported 23 Apr 2026): group sales **€899m, +7.5% constant currency** (+1.8% reported). CEO Michael Grosse **confirmed full-year 2026 guidance**: group sales growth **~5–9% cc**, underlying EBITDA margin slightly above 30%. Divisions: Bioprocess Solutions ~6–10%, Lab Products & Services ~2–6%.

The "profit warning" claim is **REFUTED**: guidance was explicitly confirmed. Press framed it as a solid start, with the soft reported number driven by FX and an underlying-net-profit dip vs prior year — consistent with the counter-claim that any disappointment was a miss-vs-consensus, not a guidance cut.

**The ~+9.6% constant-currency claim is REFUTED/mislabeled.** Q1 2026 group cc growth was **+7.5%**, not 9.6%. (9.6% does not match group, BPS at 8.1%, or LPS at 4.9%; could not source it.)

## 3. ECKERT & ZIEGLER (ETR: EUZ)

**FY2025 actuals (EQS-News):**
- Revenue: **€312.0m** (€295.8m prior year) = **+5%** ✓ matches ~€312m anchor.
- Net income: **€48.8m** vs €33.3m = **+46%** ✓
- EPS: **€0.78** (vs €0.53 split-adjusted) ✓
- Adjusted EBIT: €77.7m, +18%.

**The +46% vs +22% dispute:** Reported **net income** growth is **+46%**. The +22% figure does not match net income; it likely reflects a different metric (e.g. reported/operating EBIT or an adjusted-earnings line). **Net-income +46% CONFIRMED.**

**Segment sum:** Segments do **NOT** sum to the group total. Medical €171.3m + Isotope Products €150.1m = **€321.4m**, exceeding the €312.0m group revenue by ~€9.4m. Expected — group revenue is after intersegment eliminations — but the literal claim "segments sum to the total" is **REFUTED**.

## 4. BIOVENTIX (LON: BVXP)

**Tau/neuro revenue — both figures correct for different periods/metrics:**
- **FY2025 (year ended 30 Jun 2025):** total Tau/neuro SMA sales **~£605k** (up ~4x from £155k), split ~£530k physical antibody sales + ~£75k royalties.
- **H1 FY2026 (six months to 31 Dec 2025):** Tau/neuro **royalties** rose ~5x to **£150k** (vs £30k prior half). Half-year royalty progression: £30k → £45k → £150k.

So £605k is a full-year **total** Tau figure and £150k is a half-year **royalty-only** figure — **CONFIRMED** different periods and revenue lines. (Note: £605k is full-year total, not full-year royalties.)

**Share price / drawdown / yield (late May 2026):**
- Price **~£16.75–17.00 (1,675–1,700p)** ✓ **CONFIRMED**.
- Drawdown: **−44.5%** from a 52-week high of ~3,019p (£30.20) ✓ **CONFIRMED**.
- Dividend yield: total FY2025 dividend 150p; at 1,675p that is **~8.8–9.0%**, not 6%. The **~6% claim is REFUTED for the current price** — the 6%+ yield was accurate earlier (Oct 2025, ~£24). At today's lower price the yield has risen to ~9%.

---

**Access note:** All figures reachable via live primary and reputable secondary sources; none fabricated. The only figure not sourceable to any primary period was the Sartorius "+9.6% cc" claim.

# Concentration & Correlation Stress-Test

*Informational structural analysis — not investment advice. As of 2026-05-31.*

A 15-line portfolio looks diversified. The question this note answers is whether the **15 names are 15 bets, or a smaller number of bets wearing 15 tickers.** The conclusion, developed below, is the latter: the book is structurally closer to **4–6 independent bets**, and a large majority of it is a single stylistic wager.

---

## 0. The book at a glance

| Holding | Wt | Bucket (nominal) | Real exposure axis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Constellation Software | 12% | VMS | Serial-acquirer compounder (parent of Topicus) |
| ATOSS Software | 9% | VMS | High-multiple quality SaaS |
| Topicus | 9% | VMS | Serial-acquirer compounder (CSU-controlled) |
| Vitec Software | 6% | VMS | Serial-acquirer compounder (Nordic clone) |
| Bachem | 9% | Life-sci supply | Peptide / GLP-1 capex cycle |
| Sartorius Stedim | 6% | Life-sci supply | Bioprocessing capex cycle |
| Eckert & Ziegler | 5% | Life-sci supply | Radiopharma / isotopes |
| Bioventix | 4% | Life-sci supply | Antibody royalty (troponin/vit-D) |
| EXOR | 8% | Holdco | Family compounder, EU industrials/healthcare |
| Investor AB | 7% | Holdco | Family compounder + EQT private exposure |
| Sofina | 3% | Holdco | Private-growth / venture compounder |
| Veralto | 8% | Compliance | Water/quality "razor-blade" quality compounder |
| Secunet | 4% | Sovereignty | German state IT-security |
| iShares MSCI India | 6% | EM Asia | India beta |
| VinaCapital Vietnam VOF | 4% | EM Asia | Vietnam / frontier beta |

Nominal buckets: 5. That is the *apparent* diversification. The rest of this note is about why 5 buckets overstate the independence.

---

## 1. Correlation-cluster map — what actually moves together, and why

The portfolio resolves into a small number of **return drivers**. The clusters below are ranked by how tightly the members co-move and how concentrated the weight is.

### Cluster A — The CSU/TOI serial-acquirer ecosystem (~36% if you include all VMS; ~21% in the controlled core)
**Constellation 12% + Topicus 9%** are not two bets. CSU owns **~30% of Topicus on a fully-diluted basis** and spun it out of its own TSS group — Topicus *is* the European limb of the Constellation playbook, run by overlapping people, capital-allocation philosophy, and culture. When the market re-rates the "buy-and-hold-forever vertical software roll-up" model, these two move as one. **Vitec 6%** is a deliberate Nordic clone of the identical model. **ATOSS 9%** is a different animal mechanically (single-product organic SaaS, not a roll-up) but trades on the *same investor narrative* — German-quality, high-FCF, premium-multiple software — so it shares the multiple-compression risk even if its operations don't.
- **Shared drivers:** software-multiple regime, "quality growth" factor, EUR/CAD software sentiment, rates (long-duration cash flows).
- **Tight sub-cluster (CSU+TOI):** ownership-linked, ~21%. Effectively one company for correlation purposes.
- **Whole-cluster weight: ~36%.**

### Cluster B — The bioprocessing / GLP-1 capex cycle (~15% core, ~24% with the adjacencies)
**Bachem 9% + Sartorius Stedim 6%** ride the *same single cycle*: the build-out and subsequent digestion of biologics/peptide manufacturing capacity. Bachem is a peptide CDMO (GLP-1 tailwind and overhang); Sartorius Stedim sells the bioprocessing consumables/equipment into the same end-customers. Both already shared the 2022–2024 post-COVID destock drawdown together. This is **one wager on biomanufacturing capex**, ~15%.
**Eckert & Ziegler 5%** (radiopharma/isotopes) and **Bioventix 4%** (diagnostic antibody royalties) are *different* sub-industries — but they share the "life-sciences picks-and-shovels" factor, regulatory/reimbursement sensitivity, and the same thematic buyer base. Looser link, but not independent. **Adjacency-inclusive weight: ~24%.**

### Cluster C — Family/holdco compounders with overlapping private-market and EU-healthcare exposure (~18%)
**EXOR 8% + Investor AB 7% + Sofina 3%** are sold as three names but share a *style and look-through*:
- All three are **European family-controlled NAV-discount compounders**. They re-rate/de-rate together on the same lever: **holdco NAV-discount sentiment** (a single factor that whipsaws all three regardless of underlying assets).
- **Private-market duplication:** Investor AB's single largest "engine" exposure is its **~15% anchor stake in EQT** plus a large unlisted/Patricia Industries book; **Sofina** is essentially a fund-of-private-growth/venture vehicle. So a chunk of the "diversified holdco" sleeve is the *same* bet — private-growth/PE marks and exit windows. When private valuations and PE exit activity freeze, Investor AB *and* Sofina take the hit through the same channel.
- **EU-healthcare look-through:** EXOR (Philips, Lingotto), Investor AB (AstraZeneca, Mölnlycke), and the holdcos generally carry meaningful European healthcare — which **double-counts against Cluster B's life-sciences exposure** at the look-through level.

### Cluster D — "Defensive compliance / sovereignty" quality (~12%)
**Veralto 8% + Secunet 4%.** Thematically grouped as non-cyclical "mandated spend." Operationally unrelated (US water/quality instruments vs. German government cybersecurity), so this is the *most genuinely diversified* pair in the book — but both are still **premium-multiple quality-compounders**, so they load onto the same overarching style factor as Cluster A (see §3). Veralto also carries the same life-sciences/pharma-QC end-market that touches Cluster B.

### Cluster E — Emerging Asia beta (~10%)
**iShares MSCI India 6% + VinaCapital VOF 4%.** Genuinely the *most independent* sleeve — different macro regime, different currency (INR/VND), domestic-demand-driven, low correlation to European quality-growth. This is the one place the portfolio actually buys uncorrelated risk. But it is also the smallest conviction sleeve (10%) and India+Vietnam do share an "emerging-Asia risk-on" and USD-funding factor with each other.

### Cross-cutting overlays (the hidden, portfolio-wide correlations)

These don't live in one cluster — they cut across many holdings and are the real source of hidden concentration:

1. **Currency: EUR is the dominant exposure.** ATOSS, Bachem (CHF, EUR-linked), Sartorius Stedim, Eckert & Ziegler, Secunet, Sofina, EXOR (EUR/listed), plus Topicus' European cash flows and much of Investor AB's book are European. A rough tally puts **~55–65% of the book in EUR/CHF terms.** A EUR move, or a Europe-specific shock, hits a majority of the portfolio simultaneously. Only Veralto, the India/Vietnam sleeve, and CSU (CAD/USD) sit outside it.
2. **The "long-duration quality-growth" factor.** VMS cluster, the holdcos, Veralto/Secunet, and the high-multiple life-sci names all share sensitivity to **real long rates and the quality-growth style**. A 2022-style rate/style rotation marks down ~80% of the book through one channel.
3. **Geography: ~75%+ developed-Europe-centric** once holdco look-through is applied. The "global" feel comes mainly from two small EM lines.

---

## 2. Effective number of independent bets (vs. nominal 15)

**Approach.** The intuition: independent bets reduce risk like √N; correlated bets don't. A standard structural proxy is the **effective number of bets**

> N_eff ≈ 1 / Σ(cluster weight)²  *(a Herfindahl on independent risk clusters, the diversification analogue of HHI),*

applied **to the correlation clusters, not the line items**, because line items inside a cluster are near-redundant. I also sanity-check against a participation-ratio intuition (how the variance would actually spread if intra-cluster correlation ≈ 0.7–0.9 and cross-cluster ≈ 0.3–0.5).

**Step 1 — collapse to risk clusters (with judgment weights):**

| Cluster | Weight | Internal correlation |
|---|---|---|
| A. VMS / quality-software ecosystem | 36% | very high (CSU+TOI ~1.0; rest 0.7+) |
| B. Life-sci supply / bioprocessing | 24% | high within Bachem+Sartorius; moderate to E&Z/Bioventix |
| C. Holdco compounders | 18% | high (shared NAV-discount + private-market factor) |
| D. Compliance/sovereignty quality | 12% | low operationally, but style-linked to A |
| E. Emerging Asia | 10% | low vs. everything else (the real diversifier) |

**Step 2 — naïve HHI on these 5 clusters:**
Σw² = 0.36² + 0.24² + 0.18² + 0.12² + 0.10² = 0.1296 + 0.0576 + 0.0324 + 0.0144 + 0.0100 = **0.244**
→ N_eff ≈ 1 / 0.244 ≈ **4.1 clusters.**

**Step 3 — adjust for cross-cluster linkage (the overlays in §1).** Clusters A and D share the quality-growth style; A, B, C, D all share the EUR + long-duration-rate factor; B and C overlap on EU healthcare; C partly *is* private-growth. These positive cross-correlations mean the true N_eff is **below** the 4.1 the clean-cluster math implies. Pulling the developed-Europe-quality clusters partly together and leaving EM Asia (and, weakly, the US-listed defensives) as the only genuinely separate risk:

> **Effective independent bets ≈ 3 to 4, against a nominal 15.**

So the line-item count overstates diversification by roughly **4x**. You are paying the monitoring/complexity cost of 15 positions for the risk-spreading of ~3–4. Stated differently: of the "15," roughly **two are duplicates by ownership (CSU/TOI)**, **two more are duplicates by cycle (Bachem/Sartorius)**, **two-plus are duplicates by factor inside the holdco sleeve (Investor AB/Sofina private-markets)**, and the holdcos double-count the healthcare you already own outright.

*Assumptions shown: cluster definitions and the high/low correlation buckets are structural judgments, not estimated from a return series. The exact N_eff would shift with a real covariance matrix, but the order of magnitude — single digits, not low-teens — is robust to reasonable changes in those assumptions.*

---

## 3. The single-bet framing — how much is one wager?

Strip the labels and ask what *thesis* each holding expresses. The dominant one is:

> **"High-quality, high-return-on-capital compounders, bought at premium multiples, will keep compounding and keep their premium."**

Map the weights onto that single sentence:

- VMS ecosystem (A): **36%** — textbook expression of it.
- Holdco compounders (C): **18%** — same thesis, wrapped in a NAV discount.
- Compliance/sovereignty quality (D): **12%** — same premium-quality-compounder style.
- The high-multiple life-sci quality names (Sartorius, Bioventix, arguably Bachem): **~15% of the book** — again premium quality-growth.

That is roughly **70–80% of the portfolio expressing one stylistic wager**: *quality-compounding, in favour, at a premium multiple, financed by low long-term real rates.* The single biggest risk to the book is therefore **not** any company failing — it is the **style going out of favour** (a 2022-type quality/duration de-rating, or a sustained value/cyclical/rates regime), which would mark down ~three-quarters of the holdings *through the same mechanism, at the same time,* irrespective of business performance.

The genuinely off-thesis weight is small: **EM Asia (10%)**, plus whatever portion of the defensives/EM is truly cyclical-or-value rather than quality-growth. Call it **~10–15% of the book that is *not* the one wager.**

**Bottom line of §3: this is ~75% one bet on a single factor, dressed as five buckets.**

---

## 4. What genuine ballast would do (scenarios, not recommendations)

The portfolio is **long quality-growth, long duration, long Europe, long "things go right."** It has essentially **zero** of the asset types that historically *zig when this style zags*. The absent ballast and what each would structurally change:

**Absent entirely:**
- **Energy / real assets / broad commodities** — the natural hedge against the inflation/rates regime that is *precisely* what de-rates this book.
- **US mega-cap tech / US large-cap broad index** — the portfolio is conspicuously light on the world's largest profit pool and the USD; its "tech" is all European/Canadian small-mid software.
- **Cash / short-duration bonds** — no dry powder, no negative-beta, no convexity to buy the drawdown.
- **Gold / gold miners** — no monetary-debasement or tail hedge.
- **Broad value / cyclical equity** — nothing that *benefits* from the style rotation the book is most exposed to.

**Scenario sketches (qualitative, directional):**

- *Scenario 1 — rates/quality-growth de-rating (2022 redux).* Today: ~75% of the book marks down together through the duration channel; EM Asia (10%) provides thin offset; expect a deep, broad drawdown with little internal dispersion to cushion it. *With 10–20% ballast* (energy + short bonds + gold): the ballast is flat-to-up while equities fall, mechanically shaving the peak-to-trough by roughly its weight-times-its-divergence — turning a, say, ~35% style drawdown into something materially shallower, and crucially giving **rebalancing fuel** to buy the compounders cheap.
- *Scenario 2 — Europe-specific shock (energy, political, EUR stress).* Today: ~55–65% EUR exposure means the book takes it on the chin with few non-European offsets. *With ballast* skewed to USD assets (US tech/index, USD cash): currency and geography diversification directly dampen the hit.
- *Scenario 3 — inflation/cyclical-value regime persists for years.* Today: the book *structurally underperforms* for an extended stretch — not a crash, a grind — because its single factor is out of favour. *With ballast* in energy/real assets/value: a meaningful sleeve actively *earns* in that regime, converting a multi-year drag into something closer to flat.
- *Scenario 4 — risk-on, quality-growth rips.* Today: the book screams higher (this is the upside the concentration is buying). *With 10–20% ballast:* you give up some of that upside — that is the honest cost. Ballast is insurance; in the good state it is a drag.

The trade is explicit: **ballast lowers the ceiling a little to raise the floor a lot and to add rebalancing optionality.** Whether that trade is worth it depends on the holder's tolerance for a deep, *correlated* drawdown and a possible multi-year style winter — not on whether any company is good.

---

## 5. Blunt bottom-line — diversified or diversified-looking?

**Diversified-looking.**

- It *reads* as 15 names across 5 themes and 3 continents.
- It *behaves* as **~3–4 independent bets**, **~75% of which is a single stylistic wager** on premium-multiple quality-compounding in a benign-rates world.
- The diversification it advertises is mostly **cosmetic redundancy**: CSU/TOI are one bet by ownership (~21%); Bachem/Sartorius are one bet by cycle (~15%); the three holdcos collapse onto one NAV-discount + private-markets factor *and* double-count healthcare you already own; and ~55–65% of everything is the same EUR/long-duration trade.
- The only **genuine** diversifiers are the **~10% EM Asia sleeve** and, partially, the US-listed defensive Veralto — and they are the smallest, lowest-conviction parts of the book.

This is a **high-conviction, well-curated, single-factor portfolio.** That is a legitimate way to invest *if it is chosen on purpose* — but it should be understood as **concentration, not diversification.** The fifteen tickers are the *expression* of one view; they are not fifteen views. Anyone holding it should size it, and stress-test it, as the ~3–4-bet, ~75%-one-factor book that it actually is.

---

*Sources for factual linkages used above:*
- *Constellation Software ↔ Topicus ownership/spin-out (~30% fully-diluted): [Topicus spin-out release](https://topicus.com/news/constellation-software-inc-completes-spin-out-of-topicuscom-inc); [CSI Software](https://www.csisoftware.com/category/press-releases/2021/01/05/constellation-software-inc.-completes-spin-out-of-topicus.com-inc); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constellation_Software).*
- *Vitec as Nordic VMS serial-acquirer analogue: [Partnership Investing](https://partnershipinvesting.substack.com/p/vitec-software-vitb-ss-a-swedish).*
- *Investor AB anchor stake in EQT (~14.7%): [MarketScreener](https://www.marketscreener.com/news/anchor-shareholder-investor-ab-acquires-eqt-shares-for-sek-140-million-ce7e5edcda80f72d); [EQT AB — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EQT_AB).*

# AI vs Vertical-Market Software — Moat Tripwires

*Equity research note. Date: 2026-05-31. Portfolio context: Constellation Software (TSE: CSU), Topicus (TSXV: TOI), ATOSS Software (ETR: AOF), Vitec Software (STO: VIT-B) — ~36% combined. CSU controls TOI (~21% of book is one CSU/TOI ecosystem; treat correlated, not diversified).*

**Central question:** Does generative AI erode the moats of vertical-market / serial-acquirer software businesses?

---

## 1. Bull case vs. bear case (stated fairly)

### Bull case — why vertical, regulation-embedded systems-of-record resist AI

- **The moat is a stack, and AI hits the layers unevenly.** A useful decomposition (LongYield, 5i) splits the VMS moat into ~5 layers: (1) switching costs / data-migration pain, (2) regulatory-compliance embeddedness, (3) accumulated organizational/workflow knowledge, (4) integration into mission-critical operations, and (5) feature completeness. AI compresses development cycles (3–5 years → 12–18 months), so it attacks **layer 5 (feature parity)** hardest — but layers 1–3 are "fundamentally immune": no model instantly migrates decades of institutional data or assumes regulatory continuity.
- **Mission-critical, low-ARPU, fragmented niches are unattractive to AI-native attackers.** These are tiny TAMs (a software product for, e.g., funeral homes, dental labs, municipal water utilities, Nordic property management). The economics that protected them from horizontal SaaS protect them from AI startups too: the prize is too small, the data too specialized, the buyer too conservative.
- **Customers are conservative and slow.** ATOSS and Vitec management both describe customers wanting benefits "without major disruptions" and adopting gradually. European / government / regulated buyers move slowly — Topicus's base skews exactly this way.
- **Proprietary, workflow-embedded data is the AI *advantage*, not the vulnerability.** The incumbent that owns the system of record can layer AI on top (forecasting, automation) and deepen lock-in. ATOSS already sells AI forecasting as a *deciding factor* in hospital deals.
- **The serial-acquirer model is anti-fragile to single-vertical disruption.** CSU/Topicus hold hundreds of independent businesses across hundreds of niches. Idiosyncratic disruption in any one is diversified away, and the capital-allocation engine can redeploy FCF regardless of which verticals win. Topicus is "not just a software company; it's a capital allocator."
- **Pricing power is being defended deliberately.** Vitec is explicitly re-architecting pricing toward *value-based* models so that customer efficiency gains from AI don't translate into seat/volume revenue loss — "fair compensation for the value provided, regardless of efficiency gains."

### Bear case — where AI genuinely threatens

- **Per-seat licensing is structurally exposed.** The broad 2026 software re-rating (the "SaaS reset" / "SaaSpocalypse") is built on agentic AI cannibalizing per-seat revenue. Several of these businesses still bill on users/seats. As headcount at customers flattens or falls, seat-based recurring revenue can erode even with zero logo churn.
- **System-of-record primacy is contested.** The a16z thesis: AI collapses the distance between intent and execution, turning systems of record into a "commodity persistence tier" beneath an agent layer. If the *interface* and *workflow logic* migrate to an AI layer, the incumbent's UI/feature moat thins even where its database survives.
- **Feature-parity barriers fall fast.** The very thing that let small VMS vendors out-execute generic competitors — deep, niche feature sets built over years — can now be replicated in 12–18 months by an AI-accelerated competitor. LongYield/5i estimate **25–35% of CSU's portfolio faces meaningful AI-native disruption risk within 5 years**.
- **Pricing/index headwinds are already visible at the most price-driven name.** Vitec's organic recurring growth is guided *down* (FY25 ~3–4% → Q1'26 guide ~2–3%) on weaker index-linked price escalators — not AI per se, but it removes the cushion that masks any future volume softness.
- **Maintenance/legacy lines are in managed decline.** Perpetual-license and on-prem maintenance lines are shrinking by design (cloud transition) — fine when cloud growth more than offsets, but it narrows the margin for error if cloud growth ever stalls.

**Synthesis:** Bull and bear are not mutually exclusive. The honest framing is *which layer, which vertical, which revenue model*. Regulated systems-of-record with non-seat pricing and sticky data are well-defended; seat-priced, feature-differentiated, low-regulation niches are the soft underbelly. The portfolio sits closer to the defended end — but is not immune, and the cushions are thinning.

---

## 2. Company-by-company (latest disclosed)

> Reporting note: organic growth and FX adjustments are disclosed differently by each. CSU/TOI report a blended organic figure plus a separate organic figure for **maintenance & other recurring** (the cleanest moat gauge). ATOSS is a single-product organic grower (no M&A engine) so reported growth ≈ organic. Vitec is a serial acquirer that breaks out organic.

### Constellation Software (CSU) — FY2025 (reported 2026-03-09)
- **Organic growth:** FY2025 **+4%** total organic (~+3% ex-FX); Q4'25 **+6%** organic (~+2% ex-FX). Total revenue $11,623m (+15% incl. M&A).
- **Recurring / maintenance:** Maintenance & other recurring grew **~+6% organic on a constant-currency basis** — the key tripwire line, and it held.
- **Net retention:** Not disclosed as a single NRR figure (CSU does not report NRR).
- **Earnings quality flag:** Net income to common −30% to $512m, driven by heavy deal activity / contingent-consideration and amortization, not core operating deterioration. M&A deployment remained very heavy.
- **AI commentary:** No formal President's-letter-style AI doctrine in this cycle. Leadership transition is material: **Mark Leonard resigned for health reasons (2025-09-25), Mark Miller (COO) became President.** Paraphrased Leonard view: the moat is *staying close to customers and delivering integrated value*, not efficiency. Proprietary, workflow-embedded data positioned as an AI advantage.
- *Sources:* GlobeNewswire/Yahoo FY25 release; TipRanks; LongYield; 5i Research.

### Topicus (TOI) — FY2025 (reported 2026-02-25)
- **Organic growth:** Q4'25 and FY2025 both **+4%** total organic. Revenue €1,552.3m FY (+20% incl. M&A); Q4 €436.8m (+20%).
- **Recurring / maintenance:** Recurring revenue **€1,097m = ~71% of total**, up +22% YoY; **maintenance & recurring organic growth +6%** in both Q4 and FY — squarely in the 5–7% band held over 3 years. **No sign of AI-driven deceleration.**
- **Net retention:** Not separately disclosed.
- **FCF:** FCFA2S +23% FY to €218.7m (+40% in Q4) — capital engine intact.
- **AI commentary:** Bulls point to three numbers as the live tripwires — (1) +6% recurring organic, (2) staff-expense ratio falling to 53.8% despite heavy M&A, (3) contingent considerations rising / impairments not. Management posture: European/government conservatism + high switching costs; "capital allocator" able to pivot.
- *Sources:* GlobeNewswire/Yahoo/Globe & Mail TOI Q4'25; Expanse Stocks; Potential Multibaggers.

### ATOSS Software (AOF) — FY2025
- **Organic growth:** Group revenue **+11%** to €189.3m (effectively all organic — ATOSS is a single-product grower, not an acquirer). 20th consecutive record year.
- **Recurring / ARR:** Recurring revenue **+18%** to €131.9m (**70% of total**). Cloud & subscriptions +28% to €92.7m (**49% of revenue**). Cloud & subs ARR **+28%** to €101.3m; total ARR **~€140m (+18%)**. EBIT margin **36%**.
- **Net retention:** **~111% FY2025** (target >110%); ~112% in Q1'26; ~116% / 2.9% cloud churn at YE2024.
- **Mix shift flag:** Perpetual licenses and on-prem **maintenance declining (~−3% maintenance in Q1'26)** and hardware −24% in Q1'26 — by design, as cloud cannibalizes. Cloud growth more than offsets.
- **AI commentary:** AI forecasting / planning / automation expanding scalability; **AI forecasting a "deciding factor" in hospital wins.** AI framed as offensive product capability. FY2026 guide ~€215m revenue, EBIT margin ≥32%.
- *Sources:* ATOSS IR FY2025; Yahoo Q4'25 & Q1'26 call highlights.

### Vitec Software (VIT-B) — FY2025 / Q1'26
- **Organic growth:** Q4'25 net sales SEK 983m (+6%); **Q1'26 organic +5%** total sales (revenue SEK 955m, +9% incl. M&A, −3% FX headwind).
- **Recurring revenue:** FY2025 recurring **+11%** to SEK 3,204m; Q4'25 recurring +8% to SEK 831m; subscription gross margin **90%+**. Q1'26 **subscription organic +6%**.
- **Net retention:** Not disclosed.
- **Churn:** **~1%** (low, stable).
- **Pricing flag (the one to watch):** Organic recurring growth **guided down to ~2–3% (Q1'26) from ~3–4%** prior year, attributed to **weaker index-linked price escalators (~1% lower pricing-index uplift)** — *not* attributed to AI/churn, but it thins the cushion.
- **AI commentary:** Management acknowledges the risk that AI makes customers more efficient → fewer seats/purchases, and is **moving to value-based pricing** to decouple revenue from efficiency gains. "AI allows us to move faster than our customers, who are cautious." AI impact currently shows up in product development/growth, not cost cuts.
- *Sources:* GuruFocus/Investing.com Vitec Q3'25, Q4'25, Q1'26 call highlights; Vitec IR; Simply Wall St.

**Cross-read:** All four show recurring/maintenance organic growth in a **+5% to +6%** band (CSU/TOI maintenance +6%, ATOSS recurring +18% headline but cloud-driven, Vitec subscription organic +6%) with churn where disclosed near ~1% (Vitec) and NRR >110% where disclosed (ATOSS). **As of the latest prints, there is no quantitative fingerprint of AI erosion in the core recurring lines.** The only softening is Vitec's *price-index*-driven recurring deceleration, which is macro/contractual, not demand/churn.

---

## 3. THE KEY DELIVERABLE — Tripwire framework

Measurable thresholds that distinguish **"moats holding"** from **"moats eroding."** Watch on the cadence each company reports. The logic: AI erosion shows up *first and most cleanly* in **organic maintenance/recurring growth and gross churn**, because that line strips out M&A, FX, and one-offs and directly measures whether existing customers keep paying. Total organic growth is a noisier, lagging confirmation.

| # | Metric | Where watched | Cadence | Holding (green) | Watch (amber) | Eroding — thesis flips (red) |
|---|--------|---------------|---------|-----------------|---------------|------------------------------|
| **T1** | **Organic maintenance / recurring growth (constant-FX)** — *primary signal* | CSU & TOI "maintenance & other recurring" organic; ATOSS recurring/ARR; Vitec subscription organic | Quarterly | ≥ +4% | +1% to +4% for 2 consecutive quarters | **≤ 0% for 2+ consecutive quarters, or a >3pp decel not explained by FX/price-index** |
| **T2** | **Gross logo / revenue churn** | Vitec (~1% disclosed); ATOSS cloud churn (~3%); CSU/TOI inferred from recurring organic | Quarterly / annual | Vitec ≤1.5%; ATOSS cloud ≤4% | Vitec 1.5–2.5%; ATOSS 4–6% | **Vitec >2.5%; ATOSS cloud churn >6%; any disclosed step-up framed as competitive loss** |
| **T3** | **Net retention (where disclosed)** | ATOSS NRR (~111%) | Quarterly | ≥ 108% | 103–108% | **< 103%, or two consecutive declining prints** |
| **T4** | **Total organic growth (ex-FX)** — *confirmation, lagging* | All four | Quarterly | ≥ +3% | +1% to +3% | **≤ 0% ex-FX sustained 2+ quarters with M&A unable to offset** |
| **T5** | **M&A deployment & pricing discipline** | CSU/TOI capital deployed + implied multiples paid; deal count | Quarterly / annual | Deployment steady/rising at stable multiples | Multiples paid creeping up while deal count falls | **Deployment falls sharply (no investable targets) OR multiples paid jump materially — signals either no opportunities or chasing growth to mask organic weakness** |
| **T6** | **Maintenance / legacy attrition vs. cloud offset** | ATOSS (maintenance −3%, hardware −24% Q1'26); CSU/TOI license vs. recurring | Quarterly | Cloud/subscription growth > legacy decline by wide margin | Offset margin narrowing | **Total recurring rolls over because cloud no longer offsets legacy decline** |
| **T7** | **Price-realization / index uplift** | Vitec pricing-index commentary; ATOSS/TOI list-price actions | Annual / on calls | Index + value uplift ≥ +2% | +1% to +2% | **Sub-+1% AND accompanied by volume/seat softness (price + volume both down = real demand erosion, not just CPI)** |
| **T8** | **Seat / volume vs. value mix** | Management commentary on seat-based revenue exposure; success of value-based pricing migration | Annual | Value-based pricing migration progressing; seat exposure shrinking | Seat revenue flat while customer headcount falls | **Seat-based recurring declines YoY because customers cut seats via AI productivity (the core bear mechanism)** |
| **T9** | **Win/loss & competitive narrative** | Management Q&A; "losses to AI-native competitor" mentions | Quarterly (qualitative) | No structural-loss mentions | Isolated mentions in 1–2 verticals | **Repeated, multi-vertical mentions of losing deals/renewals to AI-native entrants** |
| **T10** | **R&D / staff-cost ratio behavior** | TOI staff-expense ratio (53.8%); all four R&D% | Annual | Falling or stable while growth holds | Rising to defend feature parity | **R&D% must rise materially just to hold organic growth (feature-parity arms race) — margin-compressing erosion** |

**How to read it as a system:**
- **Single amber = noise.** Watch, don't act.
- **Thesis genuinely flips** when **T1 goes red** (organic recurring ≤0% for 2+ quarters, FX/price-index-adjusted) **AND** it is corroborated by at least one of **T2/T3 (churn up / NRR down)** or **T9 (competitive-loss narrative).** That combination means existing customers are leaving or shrinking *because of competition*, which is the only thing that actually breaks the model.
- **Capital-allocation tripwires (T5) are the serial-acquirer-specific kill switch:** for CSU/TOI the engine matters more than any single vertical. A *collapse in deployment* (no investable VMS targets because AI is killing the asset class) or *multiple inflation to chase growth* would be a thesis-level warning even if organic stays fine for a while.
- **Vitec is the early-warning canary on pricing (T7).** It is the most price-index-dependent and already guiding recurring growth down. If that deceleration migrates from "lower CPI uplift" to "lower volume," it's the first hard datapoint of the seat/efficiency bear mechanism (T8).
- **ATOSS is the canary on mix (T6) and NRR (T3)** — it discloses the cleanest NRR and is furthest through a cloud transition, so it shows the legacy-vs-cloud offset math most transparently.

---

## 4. Verdict and uncertainty

**Where the evidence currently points (2025–2026 prints): moats HOLDING, with one yellow flag.**

- Across all four, **core recurring/maintenance organic growth sits in a +5–6% band**, churn is ~1% where disclosed (Vitec), and NRR is >110% where disclosed (ATOSS). These are precisely the lines AI erosion would hit first, and **none of them show the fingerprint.** CSU/TOI maintenance organic at +6% constant-FX is the single most reassuring datapoint, because it is the metric an AI-driven churn wave would degrade earliest.
- The serial-acquirer engines are intact: TOI FCFA2S +23%, CSU still deploying heavily. Capital allocation — the real moat for CSU/TOI — shows no stress.
- AI so far reads as **offensive** for these names (ATOSS AI forecasting winning hospital deals; incumbents layering AI on proprietary data) more than as an attacker.

**The honest yellow flag:** **Vitec's recurring-growth deceleration (~3–4% → ~2–3%).** Management attributes it to weaker price-index escalators, *not* churn — and churn is indeed still ~1%. But it removes the cushion. If a real AI/seat-efficiency effect ever arrives, it will land on a business already running thinner organic growth, and it could be hard to distinguish from the price-index story in real time. **T1 + T7 + T8 on Vitec are the highest-priority watch.**

**Explicit uncertainties / what I cannot rule out:**
1. **Lag risk.** Switching costs mean erosion would appear with a *multi-year lag* — renewals are sticky, so today's healthy recurring lines could mask attrition that only surfaces at the next 3–5 year contract cycle. Current green prints are *consistent with* but do not *prove* durable moats.
2. **Disclosure gaps.** CSU, TOI, and Vitec do **not** publish a clean NRR; we infer retention from organic recurring growth. We are partly flying on a proxy.
3. **The 25–35% exposed slice (CSU/TOI) is real but unmeasured at the line-item level** — blended organic can stay positive while a quarter of the portfolio slowly bleeds, masked by the healthy 65–75%. The blended number is reassuring but not granular.
4. **Macro vs. AI confound.** Part of the SaaS re-rating is multiple compression / rate-driven, not fundamental AI erosion. Conversely, the price-index softness at Vitec is macro/CPI, not AI. Separating the two in the data requires the corroboration logic in Section 3 (don't trust a single line).
5. **2026 prints are early.** Agentic AI's effect on seat-based billing is, by most accounts, a 2026–2028 story still unfolding. Absence of evidence today is weak evidence of absence over a 5-year horizon.

**Bottom line for a concentrated holder:** The thesis is currently *supported* by the fundamentals — these are among the better-defended corners of software (regulated, niche, sticky data, conservative buyers, non-trivial non-seat revenue, and — for CSU/TOI — a capital engine that diversifies single-vertical risk). **Stay long, but instrument the position with the Section 3 tripwires.** The thesis does not flip on narrative or share-price drawdowns; it flips on **T1 going red and being corroborated by churn/NRR or a competitive-loss narrative.** Until then, treat 2026 as a monitoring year, with Vitec's pricing/recurring line and CSU/TOI's maintenance-organic line as the two gauges to watch every quarter.

---

### Sources

## Per-name notes (noteworthy / unverifiable)

- **ATOSS Software** — Could not cleanly fix an all-time-high reference: one source cited a "€97.50 last close" and a €137 target while a USD/local quote of ~€71.8 appeared elsewhere, suggesting a recent sharp pullback or a stale feed. The fundamentals (Q1'26 rev +11%, cloud +27%, FY26 guide €215m at ≥34% EBIT) are consistent and recent. Verify the live Xetra price and ATH before using.
- **EXOR** — The discount figure is the single most-conflicted data point in this sheet: sources quoted anywhere from ~48% to ~62%. The wide range comes from different NAV reference dates (company's own FY25 NAV/share was €164.4 vs a price near €65.5, implying ~60%) versus analyst gross-asset-value methodologies. Driver is auto exposure: Stellantis ~-40% YTD 2026, Ferrari -22% TSR in 2025. Treat as "very wide and widening."
- **Investor AB** — Reported figures across sources disagreed on direction (one feed showed a "49% premium," another a ~6% discount). The 49% premium reading is almost certainly a data error; Investor AB historically trades close to NAV. Adjusted NAV was SEK 355/share at end-2025; with the B-share ~SEK 378 it is roughly at/slightly above stated NAV — i.e., little to no discount cushion. Re-verify on the IR site.
- **Vitec Software** — Price (SEK ~241.8) is from an early-April source; given the stock is ~56% off its 52-week high a fresh quote is advisable. P/E spread (17.6 vs 23.9) reflects reported vs adjusted earnings.
- **Bioventix** — One feed returned a null P/E (implying a non-standard TTM earnings figure or feed gap); Bioventix is reliably profitable, so this is a data artifact — recheck the live multiple. Price 1,875p is from 8 April; stock has been volatile (52w range 1,300-3,020p).
- **VinaCapital VOF** — Arithmetic flag: a £4.54 price against a £5.75 NAV/share is ~21% discount, yet one source stated "12.3%." The 21% figure is internally consistent with the quoted NAV; the 12.3% may be a different/older share class or date. Use the fund's own daily NAV page.
- **Sofina** — Price and NAV are from April-dated sources; an ex-dividend date of 27 May 2026 (€2.5620/share) means recent price will reflect the dividend drop. NAV ~€297/share vs price ~€223 implies ~25% discount, noting the late-2025 capital raise may have diluted NAV by €7-10/share.
- **Eckert & Ziegler** — A share split in August 2025 has corrupted some 52-week-high feeds (one showed €23.25 against a €58.80 price), so the % -from-high could not be cleanly verified. Fundamentals (FY25 rev €312m +5.4%, Q1'26 +7.0%) are current.
- **Secunet & Sartorius** — Clean trailing P/E could not be verified from the searched sources (both have been through earnings volatility / de-rating); flagged as n/a rather than estimated.

*Sources are primary quote/IR pages where available; several rows rely on stockanalysis.com / Simply Wall St aggregations for the most recent close. All NAV-discount and "% from high" figures should be re-confirmed against the issuer's own data before any decision.*

# Building the "Best-15" — Keep / Replace Synthesis

_Compiled 2026-05-31. Informational synthesis of the 20-lane gem hunt against the current book. NOT financial advice — every decision is yours. Treat valuations as late-May-2026 and re-verify before acting._

## The framing
You **love this book and want to keep exactly 15 names.** This is not a teardown — it's "make the best version of it." Two things are simultaneously true (per the stress-test + honest-read):
1. The businesses are genuinely high quality.
2. The book is **diversified-looking, not diversified** — ~3–4 effective bets, ~75% one stylistic wager (premium quality-compounding in a benign-rates world), with **no energy/real assets, no financials, no US, EUR-heavy**.

The elegant result of the hunt: **the highest-conviction gems also happen to fill those exact blind spots.** So upgrading quality and de-monolithing the book is often the *same move*.

---

## The current 15 (anchor)
| # | Holding | Wt | Verdict from the hunt |
|---|---------|----|----|
| 1 | Constellation Software | 12% | **Core keep** — the engine; no replacement proposed |
| 2 | ATOSS Software | 9% | **Keep** — moat intact (AI-tripwires green: NRR ~111%, organic +11%) |
| 3 | Topicus | 9% | **Keep** (note: CSU controls it → ~21% is one ecosystem) |
| 4 | Bachem | 9% | **Keep** — but bioprocessing/GLP-1 cycle overlaps Sartorius |
| 5 | Veralto | 8% | **Keep** (BMI is a cheaper/higher-ROIC alternative if ever swapped) |
| 6 | EXOR | 8% | **Keep** — but holdco cluster overlaps Investor AB + Sofina |
| 7 | Investor AB | 7% | **Keep** — highest-quality holdco |
| 8 | Vitec | 6% | **Keep** (Nabtesco/Addnode are alternatives) |
| 9 | Sartorius Stedim | 6% | **Top replace candidate** — bioprocessing overlap w/ Bachem; recovery 50–70x P/E |
| 10 | **iShares MSCI India ETF** | 6% | **#1 replace** — passive, no moat, no alignment (nominated by ~14 lanes) |
| 11 | Eckert & Ziegler | 5% | **Keep** — radiopharma idiosyncratic (IBA complements it) |
| 12 | Secunet | 4% | **Keep (validated)** — or upgrade to Advenica (purer high-assurance) |
| 13 | Bioventix | 4% | **Replace candidate** — tiny single-product; 2032 troponin cliff |
| 14 | **VinaCapital Vietnam** | 4% | **#2 replace** — passive EM basket; FPT is the single best name inside it |
| 15 | Sofina | 3% | **Replace candidate** — duplicates private-market exposure (Investor AB's EQT) |

**Lowest-regret swaps (consensus):** India ETF, VinaCapital Vietnam. **Optional quality upgrades:** Sartorius, Bioventix, Sofina, one holdco. **Validated keeps:** Constellation, ATOSS, Topicus, Bachem, Veralto, EXOR, Investor AB, Vitec, Eckert & Ziegler, Secunet.

---

## Three options, by how much you want to change

### OPTION A — Minimal change (2 swaps): retire the two passive sleeves
The single highest-confidence, lowest-controversy upgrade. Replace the only two non-stock-picked positions with active compounders.

| Out | In | Why | Fills |
|---|---|---|---|
| iShares MSCI India 6% | **Topaz Energy (TPZ.TO)** or **Aurubis (NDA.DE)** | Real-asset/royalty ballast — the #1 missing piece; behaves uncorrelated to quality-growth | Energy/real assets, non-EUR ballast |
| VinaCapital Vietnam 4% | **OTC Markets (OTCM)** or **Kinsale (KNSL)** | US financial-infrastructure tollbooth / E&S insurer; founder-aligned | US/USD, financials |

_Keeps the book ~entirely intact while adding the two asset classes it most lacks. If you'd rather keep EM exposure: India ETF → **Polycab** (founder-led India compounder), Vietnam → **FPT** (best Vietnam franchise; check VOF double-counting)._

### OPTION B — Moderate (4 swaps): A + reduce the correlated clusters
Also retire the bioprocessing overlap (Bachem AND Sartorius ride one GLP-1/bioprocessing cycle = ~15% as one bet) and the smallest single-product name.

| Out | In | Why |
|---|---|---|
| iShares MSCI India 6% | **Topaz Energy** (energy ballast) | as above |
| VinaCapital Vietnam 4% | **OTC Markets** or **Kinsale** (US financials) | as above |
| Sartorius Stedim 6% | **DISCO (6146.T)** | breaks the bioprocessing double-bet; adds Japan + a 45–54% ROIC monopoly; ⭐ the only gem chosen by two lanes |
| Bioventix 4% | **LeMaitre Vascular (LMAT)** | larger, more liquid, equally moaty US medtech; less single-product risk |

### OPTION C — Full de-monolith (5 swaps): B + thin the holdco cluster
Collapse the three-holdco overlap (EXOR + Investor AB + Sofina share European healthcare + private-growth; Investor AB's EQT stake duplicates Sofina).

| Out | In | Why |
|---|---|---|
| (Options B four, plus) | | |
| Sofina 3% | **Eurofins (ERF.PA)** or **Aurubis (NDA.DE)** | the most venture-/private-overlapped holdco → a founder-led TIC compounder at 9x EV/EBITDA, or Europe's copper toll-road; keeps a "capital-allocator/real-asset" flavour without the duplication |

_(If you'd rather keep all three holdcos, leave Sofina and stop at Option B.)_

---

## Illustrative "Best-15" (Option C construction)
One concrete, internally-consistent example — _not_ a prescription. Weights keep your top-heavy conviction shape while seating the new exposures meaningfully.

| # | Holding | Wt | Cluster | Status |
|---|---------|----|---------|--------|
| 1 | Constellation Software | 12% | VMS | keep |
| 2 | ATOSS Software | 9% | VMS | keep |
| 3 | Topicus | 8% | VMS | keep (trim 1%) |
| 4 | Bachem | 8% | Life-sci | keep (trim 1%) |
| 5 | Veralto | 8% | Defensive/quality | keep |
| 6 | EXOR | 7% | Holdco | keep (trim 1%) |
| 7 | Investor AB | 7% | Holdco | keep |
| 8 | **DISCO** | 7% | Japan hard-tech | **new** (← Sartorius) |
| 9 | Vitec | 6% | VMS | keep |
| 10 | **Topaz Energy** | 6% | Energy/real assets | **new** (← India ETF) |
| 11 | Eckert & Ziegler | 5% | Radiopharma | keep |
| 12 | **OTC Markets** | 5% | US financials | **new** (← VinaCapital Vietnam) |
| 13 | **LeMaitre Vascular** | 4% | US medtech | **new** (← Bioventix) |
| 14 | Secunet | 4% | Defensive/sovereignty | keep |
| 15 | **Eurofins** | 4% | TIC/quality | **new** (← Sofina) |

**Total 100% — 15 holdings.**

What this version changes structurally:
- **Adds the 3 missing asset classes:** energy/real assets (Topaz), financials (OTC Markets), and broadens hard-tech beyond software (DISCO).
- **Geography:** EUR weight drops; adds Japan (DISCO), Canada (Topaz), and US (OTC Markets, LeMaitre). Less monolithically European.
- **Cuts the two biggest hidden correlations:** removes the Bachem+Sartorius single-cycle double-bet (→ DISCO) and thins the 3-holdco/private-market overlap (Sofina → Eurofins).
- **Keeps the soul of the book:** owner-operators, recurring revenue, high ROIC, durable moats. Every new name is founder/family-aligned or moat-anchored (DISCO/Sekiya, Topaz/Tourmaline-anchored, OTCM/Coulson ~30%, LeMaitre family, Eurofins/Martin 66% votes).

Effective independent bets rise from ~3–4 toward ~6–7; the "one-factor" share falls from ~75% toward ~55–60%.

---

## If you want to keep it EM-flavoured instead
Prefer to retain emerging-market upside rather than pivot the freed slots to US/energy? The strongest single-name EM replacements:
- India ETF → **Polycab** (4.5/5) or **CDSL** (4/5) or **CAMS** (3.5/5, fills financials too)
- Vietnam VOF → **FPT** (4/5) or **Kaspi.kz** (4/5, fills financials + frontier; size ~4–5% for sanctions tail)

This keeps your EM weight but swaps passive baskets for founder-led franchises — though it does **not** fix the energy/financials/US blind spots (Options A–C do).

---

## Watch-list (high quality, wrong price/time today — revisit)
Lasertec (EUV-inspection monopoly, order trough), Vicor (AI power, post-run), BESI (hybrid-bonding, 75x), Arteche (+339%), Exail (+370%), Renishaw (semi inflection — arguably already a buy at ~18–20x fwd), Aurubis (copper, capex inflection 2028/29), Quálitas (LatAm insurer, VAT transition), RENK (defense, de-rated), Kaspi (sanctions overhang), Cognex/Harmonic Drive (price-dependent), Voltamp (promoter-stake question), ChemoMetec (verify recent −29% week).

## The single decision that still matters most
None of the above changes the **AI-vs-VMS question** (~36% of the book — Constellation/ATOSS/Topicus/Vitec). The tripwires in `research/ai-vs-vertical-software-moat-tripwires.md` are the live monitoring framework; current reads are green (organic recurring +6%, ATOSS NRR ~111%, churn ~1–3%). Watch those, not headlines.

---

### How to use this file
This is a menu, not a verdict. Pick an option (A/B/C or the EM-flavoured variant), tell me which slots/names you want, and I'll turn the chosen construction into a maintained target-portfolio file with rationale, position-sizing notes, and per-name monitoring tripwires.

## Emerging swap thesis (preliminary — pending final 4 lanes + your input)

The data points to a clean way to **upgrade the book to "best-15" while fixing the blind spots the stress-test flagged**, mostly by retiring the two passive/weakest sleeves:

1. **Replace iShares MSCI India ETF** → a single active compounder. Top candidates: **Polycab** (cleanest), **CDSL**, or **OTC Markets** (if you'd rather the slot fill the US/financials blind spot than stay India).
2. **Replace VinaCapital Vietnam** → **Topaz Energy** (energy/real-asset ballast — fills the single biggest blind spot) or **nVent / InterDigital** (US, AI buildout).
3. **Optional quality upgrades within clusters** (only if conviction is deliberate): **DISCO** or **Hansol** in place of **Sartorius**; **LeMaitre** or **Hamamatsu** in place of **Bioventix**; **Advenica** alongside/﻿in place of **Secunet**; **Eurofins/Aurubis** in place of a **holdco** (Sofina/EXOR/Investor AB).

The recurring theme: the gems that score highest *also* happen to fix the EUR-heavy, no-ballast, no-financials, no-US problems — DISCO/Lasertec/Hamamatsu (Japan), OTC Markets/Vicor/Kinsale/IBP (US), Topaz/PrairieSky (energy), Aurubis (copper), Quálitas (LatAm financials).
