# European & Nordic Serial Acquirers / Compounders — Hidden Gems Lane

**Analyst Date: 31 May 2026 | Scope: Beyond the already-owned (Topicus, Vitec) and the ultra-famous (Lifco, Indutrade, Halma)**

## Executive Summary
After screening the full Nordic/European serial-acquirer universe (~30 listed names on Nasdaq Stockholm alone, plus UK and Continental peers), three genuinely compelling ideas stand out at current prices as of late May 2026. A fourth (Lagercrantz) is high quality but not a buy today. The field is more crowded and discovered than in 2020–22; the easy money has been made on the obvious names. The remaining gems require either (a) accepting a turnaround narrative, (b) a complexity discount the market hasn't closed, or (c) structural underpricing vs obvious peers.

## Shortlist
| # | Name | Ticker | Country | ~Mkt Cap | Stage |
|---|------|--------|---------|----------|-------|
| 1 | Teqnion AB | TEQ.ST | Sweden | SEK 2.7bn (~€230m) | Small-cap turnaround compounder |
| 2 | Addnode Group | ANOD-B.ST | Sweden | SEK 6.3bn (~€540m) | Mid-cap software acquirer, mispriced vs peers |
| 3 | Momentum Group | MMGR-B.ST | Sweden | SEK 6.0bn (~€510m) | Newly independent distributor/acquirer, down ~30% TTM |
| 4 | Lagercrantz | LAGR-B.ST | Sweden | SEK ~56bn | Mature high-quality — watch-list only (fully valued) |

---

## GEM 1: TEQNION AB (TEQ.ST)
- **Ticker:** TEQ.ST (Nasdaq First North Growth Market, Stockholm) | **Country:** Sweden (+ growing UK/Ireland) | **Mkt cap:** SEK ~2.7bn (~€230m) | **Stage:** Post-stumble turnaround compounder, operationally inflecting.

**Structural wave & why under-covered:** Industrial niche consolidation — buying owner-managed Swedish/UK industrial businesses (SEK 25–150m revenue, 15–25%+ EBITA margins) before they go to PE. Decades-long wave: average Nordic owner is 58; succession is perennial; PE ignores sub-€10m EBIT. Severely under-covered because (a) it trades on First North Growth Market (limited index/institutional access), (b) it had a high-profile stumble in 2023–24 when organic growth went negative and margins collapsed, (c) founder-CEO holds only a mid-single-digit stake (no dynasty anchor). International investors have never heard of it.

**Moat:** Decentralised operating model with ~8-person HQ; subsidiaries keep brand/staff/management (critical to win sellers who fear PE integration). Pays ~4–5x EBITA (verified: Cambs Compressor Feb 2026 at 4.1x; Birkett Bogmats, HT Servo all sub-5x). UK/Ireland expansion (TEQ Väst) at 26% EBITA margin. Subsidiaries supply mission-critical industrial consumables/precision components/motion control where switching isn't worth it.

**Ownership:** Johan Steene (CEO since 2009) ~5% (~SEK 133m), bought 100k shares at SEK 202 in 2024 on the dip. Daniel Zhang (Deputy/CXO, ex-McKinsey/Bain) heir-apparent. Woodlock House Family Capital (Chris Mayer, author of *100 Baggers*) a major external holder — quality signal. No dynasty/family control (risk + opportunity).

**Economics (sourced):** Revenue FY2025 SEK 1.80bn (+15%). EBITA margin ~11% FY2025; Q1 2026 14.3% (record). Organic growth Q1 2026 −5.4% (deliberately exiting unprofitable Nordic legacy revenue). TEQ Väst EBITA margin ~26%; TEQ Nord 11.6% in Q1 2026 (recovering from 5%). 9 acquisitions in 2025 adding SEK ~191m sales and ~SEK 50m EBT (~25% EBT margin). EV/EBITDA ~9.1x annualised Q1 / 16.6x TTM. Net debt SEK ~336m (~1.6x, below 2.5x target). Credit facility expanded SEK 575m→1bn in 2026. ROIC implied ~25% on deal price (aggregate unverified). FCF historically weak in transition; Q1 2026 inflecting. EPS FY2025 SEK 5.74.

**Valuation:** Share SEK ~157; mkt cap SEK 2.68bn; EV ~SEK 3.1bn. EV/EBITDA ~9.1x annualised vs 16.6x TTM (gap = margin inflection). Fwd P/E ~28x on depressed earnings; if EBITA recovers to 9%+ on SEK 1.8–2.0bn revenue → EBITA SEK 162–180m → fwd EV/EBITA ~17–19x. Cheap vs Lifco 30x+, Addtech 25x+.

**10-yr bull case:** Doubles EPS every 5 yrs (15% CAGR target). UK platform → 50%+ of revenue at 25%+ EBITA; Nordic legacy improves/rationalised; facility deployed at 4–5x on 20–25% EBITA deals. By 2036: SEK 5–7bn revenue, ~18% EBITA = ~SEK 900m EBITA; at 20x = SEK 18bn vs SEK 2.7bn today = **6–7x** base, **8–10x** at 25x.

**Risks:** (1) FCF/working-capital discipline — 2023–24 stumble was partly self-inflicted (bad inventory mgmt); if turnaround stalls you're paying 16x for stagnant earnings. (2) UK execution — TEQ Väst small (~5 subs). (3) No dynasty anchor; Steene is 52, succession not guaranteed.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **iShares MSCI India** — direct exposure to the same SME-succession wave that drives Constellation/Vitec/Topicus, earlier-stage, less covered, far cheaper. A structural-model bet, not a geographic bet. **Conviction: 4/5** — strongest risk/reward in this lane now.

---

## GEM 2: ADDNODE GROUP (ANOD-B.ST)
- **Ticker:** ANOD-B.ST (Nasdaq Stockholm Main) | **Country:** Sweden (global ops) | **Mkt cap:** SEK ~6.3bn (~€540m) | **Stage:** Mature mid-cap software compounder at a deep discount to Nordic software peers.

**Structural wave & why under-covered:** Serial acquirer of mission-critical niche software at the intersection of (1) AEC/BIM digitalisation, (2) PLM for industrial manufacturers, (3) Nordic public-sector digital transformation — regulatory/productivity-driven, multi-decade. Awkward classification gap (too industrial for tech analysts, too software for capital-goods desks). ABG upgraded to BUY Feb 2025 noting a 28% discount to Nordic M&A compounder peers vs a 12% 5-yr-avg discount.

**Moat:** #1 global Autodesk Solution Provider (Symetri + Microdesk) — Autodesk deeply embedded in every construction firm; switching requires retraining thousands of engineers. Process Management division serves Nordic municipalities (near-infinite switching costs). 63% recurring revenue (2025). 84 acquisitions since 2003, 90%+ claimed success, 8-person HQ; pipeline of 50+ targets. SolidCAD acquired at implied ~5–7x.

**Ownership:** Aretro Capital (Staffan Hanstorp, co-founder) ~17.5% votes / ~5.4% capital; dual-class anchor. Institutions: Alecta 12.2%, Swedbank Robur 9.8%, SEB 7.1%, AP4 5.4%.

**Economics:** Revenue FY2025 SEK 5.79bn; EBITA SEK 903m (15.6% margin, up from 11.1% in 2024); Q4 2025 EBITA margin 19.1%. Recurring 63%. 10 acquisitions in 2025 (+SEK ~700m sales). EV/EBIT 13.3x; P/E 15.4x; EV/EBITDA 9.3x. Net cash position SEK ~2.23bn (verify treatment of leases/deferred consideration). ROIC 8.79% (goodwill-laden — bear case); ROE 15.0%. New targets: EBITA growth ≥15%, margin ≥17%, ND ≤2.5x EBITDA.

**Valuation:** P/E 15.4x trailing; EV/EBITDA 9.3x — anomalously cheap for a software compounder (Vitec ~25–35x, Topicus ~40–50x, Lagercrantz ~30–35x). Discount drivers: Autodesk dependency, US integration drag, goodwill-suppressed ROIC, low media profile.

**10-yr bull case:** EBITA margin to 17–20% as US matures and recurring mix rises; 12–15% revenue CAGR funded by net cash. By 2035: ~SEK 20bn revenue, 18% EBITA = SEK 3.6bn; at 20x = SEK 72bn vs SEK 6.3bn today = **11x**, more on re-rating.

**Risks:** (1) Autodesk disintermediation (going direct-to-customer). (2) US integration drag 2–3 yrs. (3) Reported ROIC may not recover if deals overpriced.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** or complement **Vitec** — broader, cheaper, more global version of Vitec, plus physical-world digitalisation (BIM/PLM). **Conviction: 3.5/5.**

---

## GEM 3: MOMENTUM GROUP (MMGR-B.ST)
- **Ticker:** MMGR-B.ST | **Country:** Sweden | **Mkt cap:** SEK ~6.0bn (~€510m) | **Stage:** Independent since 2022 (spun from Alligo); industrial distributor/acquirer, down ~30% TTM.

**Structural wave & why under-covered:** (1) Industrial aftermarket parts consolidation (Momentum ~5% of fragmented Nordic market = decades of runway); (2) predictable MRO demand (non-discretionary). Under-covered: only independent since 2022, recent Nordstjernan mandatory-bid uncertainty, −30% price drop scared momentum capital, "boring distribution" bucket.

**Moat:** 750,000+ SKU inventory (90+ years to build); mission-critical replacement parts where delivery speed > price; **EBITA/Working Capital 58%** (hallmark of distribution pricing power); only ~5% Sweden / ~2% other Nordics share = huge runway; acquires at 5–7x EBITA (6 deals 2025, +SEK ~300m); 3-person HQ (Constellation-style).

**Ownership:** Nordstjernan (Ax:son Johnson family) ~15% at listing, selling down but significant. CEO Ulf Lilius bought 97,470 B shares avg SEK 109.83 (~SEK 10.7m). Hedelius/Börjesson families hold A-shares (voting control).

**Economics:** Revenue FY2025 SEK 3.10bn (+8%); EBITA SEK 337m (+5%); margin 10.9% (target 12–13%+); organic −2% (industrial slowdown); EBITA/WC 58%; EPS SEK 3.80; net debt SEK 344m; equity/assets 36%. Mkt cap SEK ~6.0bn; share ~SEK 121 (−30% TTM). P/E ~32x; EV/EBITA ~18.8x. Doubled EBITA target within 4 yrs of listing, 1 yr early.

**10-yr bull case:** Cycle recovers; organic 3–5%; acquisitions +5–10%/yr; margins to 13%+. By 2035: SEK 8–10bn revenue, 13% margin = SEK 1.0–1.3bn EBITA; at 18x = SEK 18–23bn vs SEK 6bn = **3–4x** base, 4–5x at 22x.

**Risks:** (1) Organic growth deficit if Nordic industry stagnates; rising acquisition multiples. (2) Nordstjernan overhang (selling). (3) E-commerce disruption of distributors (10-yr).

**Portfolio fit:** Blind-spot filler — no Nordic industrial MRO/distribution in the 15. Could take from **VinaCapital Vietnam**. **Conviction: 3/5.**

---

## WATCH-LIST: LAGERCRANTZ (LAGR-B.ST)
Revenue FY2025/26 SEK 10.6bn (+13%); EBITA margin 18.1%→20%; ROE 29%; 8 acquisitions + 4 post-year-end; EPS +18% to SEK 5.81. P/E sources conflict (30–36x quoted vs derived ~40–50x trailing — **flagged**). Dispersed ownership, no family anchor. Superb but priced for perfection; needs 15%+ EPS growth forever. Not a hidden gem.

## Screened but rejected
- **Röko (ROKO-B.ST):** EV/EBITA ~27x, P/E ~37x; ROIC only 11.9%; Karlsson's Lifco pedigree priced in; put/call liabilities >25% EBITA. Watch for 30–40% drawdown.
- **Sdiptech:** EBITA margin 21–27% but ROIC 5.92% (goodwill discipline questions); divestment programme ongoing.
- **Judges Scientific (JDG.L):** Pays ~5x EBIT for instrument makers, ~25% margin; but well-covered in UK, US research-funding cuts a headwind.
- **Cicor (CICN.SW):** EMS manufacturer, EBITDA margin only 10.9%; not a pricing-power acquirer.
- **Storskogen:** structural leverage problems, avoid.

## Replacement matrix
| Gem | Replace | Rationale |
|-----|---------|-----------|
| Teqnion (4/5) | India ETF | Same long-duration compounding thesis, earlier-stage, cheaper; structural-model bet not geographic |
| Addnode (3.5/5) | India ETF / add to software cluster | 15x P/E vs 30–40x for same model |
| Momentum (3/5) | blind-spot filler / small from VOF | No Nordic MRO exposure in book |

**Single most compelling action:** initiate **Teqnion** to replace the passive India ETF.


<<<<< FILE: research/gems/02-japan-niche-leaders.md >>>>>

# Japanese Hidden Global Niche Leaders

**Date: 2026-05-31 | For a concentrated 15-stock long-term portfolio**

## Executive Summary
Four candidates emerge after adversarial filtering. One — Taiyo Holdings — has been privatized by KKR (announced March 2026) and is removed from the investable set. Of the rest, two clear the bar: **Disco (6146.T)** and **Hamamatsu Photonics (6965.T)**. **Harmonic Drive (6324.T)** is a credible watch-list name facing Chinese-competition moat erosion. **NOF (4403.T)** is an interesting mRNA/DDS picks-and-shovels play but its life-science segment is small and opaque — a monitor.

Note: Japan adds a new geography (none of the 15 are Japanese), and Japan's governance-reform tailwind (buybacks >¥10tn FY2024, cross-shareholding unwind) is a secular re-rating backdrop.

---

## GEM 1: DISCO CORPORATION
- **Ticker:** 6146.T (Tokyo) / ADR DSCSY | **Country:** Japan | **Mkt cap:** ~¥7.1–7.9tn (~$47–52B) | **Stage:** Mature compounder, secular re-acceleration.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Makes dicing saws, grinding wheels, laser saws — the final mechanical step that cuts wafers into chips and thins them. Three converging waves: (1) advanced packaging (HBM4, chiplets, hybrid bonding — HBM stacks ground <50µm); (2) SiC power-semi boom (EV/industrial) — SiC is far harder, needing Disco's specialized grinding; (3) consumables flywheel (blades/wheels ~20–35% of sales, repurchased regularly, scales with installed base). Under-covered: mostly Japanese-domestic coverage; the "Will" internal-currency culture is misread by Western analysts; consumables moat looks like "hardware."

**Moat:** ~70–80% global share in dicing saws and grinders (multiple sources). Blade-machine co-engineering — switching means re-qualifying all dicing recipes (months of downtime, yield risk at TSMC/Samsung/Intel/SK Hynix/Micron). Near-lockdown on SiC. "Will" internal market (employees bid for work via virtual currency) drove ~20pp operating-margin expansion since 2015 — a non-replicable organizational moat.

**Ownership:** Founded 1937 by Mitsuo Sekiya; CEO Kazuma Sekiya is the grandson. Sekiya family ~13.8%. Institutional-quality governance.

**Economics (FY ending Mar):** Revenue FY2025 ¥393.3bn (+27.9%); FY2026 ¥436.9bn (+11.1%). Gross margin ~70%; operating margin ~42%; net margin ~31%. ROIC ~45–54% (methodology-dependent). Net cash ¥284.6bn, zero debt. FCF/share 10-yr CAGR >15% (directional).

**Valuation:** P/E ~52x TTM / ~40–43x fwd; P/FCF ~72x; EV/EBITDA ~34x. Rich on headline, but at 45%+ ROIC and net cash the earnings power is understated. Episodic ~25–30% drawdowns (2024 China/cycle fears) offer entries.

**10-yr bull case:** Humanoid robots, advanced packaging, SiC, wafer-level optics expand TAM. If consumables mix → 30%+ and advanced-packaging tools → 25%+ of revenue, operating leverage drives earnings to 3–5x at mid-single-digit revenue CAGR.

**Risks:** (1) Semi-capex cyclicality (revenue/profit can fall 30–40%). (2) China export-control escalation (~20–35% China exposure; domestic alternatives promoted). (3) Demanding valuation — outsized de-rating on any miss.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Sartorius Stedim** (comparable picks-and-shovels logic, superior ROIC, AI/semi wave vs biotech-capex cycle) or **India ETF**. **Conviction: 4/5.** ⭐ Cross-confirmed by the semicap lane.

---

## GEM 2: HAMAMATSU PHOTONICS
- **Ticker:** 6965.T / OTC HPHTF | **Country:** Japan | **Mkt cap:** ~¥831bn (~$5.5B) | **Stage:** Deep-quality trough; entering earnings recovery.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** World near-monopoly in photomultiplier tubes (PMTs — single-photon detectors) and a global leader in opto-semiconductors/imaging sensors. Waves: photon-based semi inspection (next-node, EUV mask), AI/quantum hardware, proton therapy & PET expansion, neutrino-detection infrastructure. ~$5.5B company, only 9 analyst ratings (mostly domestic), 62x PE reflecting a cyclical trough — FY2025 operating profit fell 50% as semi + medical-bio demand fell together. FY2026 inflection: May-2026 raised guidance — sales +9.4%, operating profit +23.7% (H2-weighted).

**Moat:** >90% global PMT share (essentially a monopoly in large-area PMTs; no credible competitor in giant PMTs for neutrino physics). 72% of revenue outside Japan (US ~26%, Europe ~24%). PMTs in PET, radiation detectors, CERN/Super-Kamiokande, oil logging, LIDAR. Proprietary SiPM tech; ~10–12% R&D intensity (directional). Expanding into OLED/wafer inspection and AI/quantum photonics.

**Ownership:** Founded 1953; Akira Hiruma (son of Teruo) chairs; family low-single-digit stake but dominant cultural influence. Institutions: BlackRock ~6.5%, Vanguard ~5.3%; ~10% insiders.

**Economics (sourced):** Revenue FY2025 ¥212.1bn; FY2026 guidance ¥232.0bn (+9.4%). Op profit FY2025 ¥16.2bn (7.6% margin), FY2026 +23.7%. Gross margin TTM 47.3%. Trough ROE 4.3% / ROIC 3.4% (cyclical, not structural; prior-peak operating margin ~21%). Net positive cash. Mgmt FY2026 op profit ~¥20bn implies ROIC recovery to mid-teens.

**Valuation:** PE 63x TTM / 52x fwd (on depressed earnings); P/S 3.8x (modest for a 47%-GM quasi-monopoly); EV/EBITDA 21x. Through-cycle normalized PE 20–35x; recovery re-rates the stock.

**10-yr bull case:** PMT demand grows with neutrino physics, proton therapy rollout, quantum-optics/photonic-chip inspection. Opto-semi benefits from EUV mask inspection, AI sensor fusion. Medical-bio recovery brings margins back toward 15–20% operating. Conservative culture → upside flows to buybacks/dividends.

**Risks:** (1) Prolonged medical-bio/semi weakness delays recovery (H2-loaded FY2026). (2) SiPM substitution (Broadcom/ON Semi) over 5–10 yrs (partly mitigated by own SiPM). (3) China ~20% revenue / export-restriction risk.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Bioventix** — broader photonic infrastructure across medical/semi/scientific vs single-product royalty. **Conviction: 4/5.** _(Note: the semicap lane was cooler on Hamamatsu given trough ROIC.)_

---

## WATCH-LIST: HARMONIC DRIVE SYSTEMS (6324.T)
- ~$4B cap. World-dominant strain-wave ("harmonic") gears for small/medium robot joints; humanoid wave real (Tesla Optimus 10,000 units Mar 2026; Figure in BMW; Chinese humanoid scaling — 12–30 actuators each = 5–10x TAM). FY2026 guide 14% sales growth, 9% op margin; 3-yr target ¥90bn sales / ¥15bn op profit.
- **Held back:** Jefferies 2025 downgrade flagged Chinese competition (Beijing CTKM, Leaderdrive) gaining in collaborative/humanoid; current op margin ~3% TTM, ROIC ~1%, 3-yr avg net margin negative (recovery forecast not demonstrated); ~$4B already prices a humanoid ramp (~10x sales); no family/founder alignment. **Verdict:** revisit when op margin sustainably >10% and humanoid volumes disclosed. **2/5 now.**

## FILED: TAIYO HOLDINGS (NOT INVESTABLE)
Global #1 PCB solder resist (>70% liquid SR, ~100% BT-substrate SR), HBM tailwind, 23% op margin, 19% ROIC — strongest pure gem in the lane, BUT KKR ¥528.6bn (~$3.3B) tender at ¥4,750/share announced March 2026 (close ~Oct 2026). Now a risk-arb, not a compounder.

## FILED: NOF CORP (MONITOR)
World-leading PEG/ionizable lipids for mRNA/LNP delivery (COATSOME SS), plus surfactants/specialty chemicals/explosives. ~$4.1B cap, PE ~19x, net margin 15.7%, ROI 14.1%. DDS/life-science revenue not separately disclosed at sufficient granularity. Not gem-grade until segment-level DDS revenue is disclosed and >20% of total.

## Dismissed: Mitsui High-tec (6966.T)
70% global EV motor-core share (real moat) but op margins compressed to 4.7% (FY2026), op profit −18.8% despite +11% revenue; Toyota concentration. Not strong enough to displace a holding.

## Final
Add **Disco** (replace Sartorius) and **Hamamatsu** (replace Bioventix). Both Japan-based — adds a new geography with a governance-reform re-rating tailwind.

# Korea & Taiwan Hidden Supply-Chain Gems

**Lane:** niche semiconductor materials/equipment/components — picks-and-shovels into the AI/HBM/advanced-packaging supercycle, under-covered by Western investors.

## Pre-screen eliminations
- **Hanmi Semiconductor (042700.KS, ~$20B):** Brilliant TC Bonder franchise (43% op margin, 55% founder-family ownership, Kwak Dongshin buying) but no longer under-covered or a monopoly — SK Hynix diversifying to ASMPT, Micron to BESI for HBM4; Hanmi share at SK Hynix reportedly falling to 20–30% by 2026; P/B 42x, EV/EBITDA 132x on peak margins. Moat cracking at peak valuation. Not a gem at this price.
- **Chroma ATE (2360.TW, ~$31B):** NVIDIA's exclusive system-level test supplier for GB200; 61–65% GM, ROE 29%, +31% revenue 2025. But +633% in 52 weeks, P/E ~80–100x, EV/FCF ~536x. Fully discovered. Not hidden.
- **Kinsus (3189.TW, ~$11.5B):** ABF substrate AI beneficiary (+29% 2025 revenue, net profit +31x) but op margins only 6.8% (vs 22.6% 2022 peak) = severe cyclicality; Pegatron-affiliated governance; 9 buys already; fwd P/E ~27x. Covered, cyclical, thin margins.

---

## GEM 1: LEENO INDUSTRIAL
- **Ticker:** 058470.KQ (KOSDAQ) | **Country:** South Korea | **Mkt cap:** ~₩8.4tn (~$6.1B) | **Stage:** Mature niche monopolist, AI-driven re-acceleration.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Makes ultra-precision test pins (probe pins) — the spring-loaded needles contacting wafers/die during electrical test. Re-rated by: (1) AI logic complexity (more I/O points, finer pitch — Blackwell <75µm); (2) HBM test intensity (more stacking layers = more test touchpoints); (3) 3nm/2nm ramp tightening specs (pins as thin as 0.075mm). Under-covered: KOSDAQ (not KOSPI), ~$190M revenue, no ADR, files in Korean, Busan-HQ, ~zero Western coverage. Investors think test = Cohu/Teradyne/Advantest and miss the consumable pin layer.

**Moat:** >70% global share in wafer-test ultra-precision probe pins ("Leeno Pin" = generic noun). Prohibitive tolerances; qualified into 1,000+ fabs (Samsung, SK Hynix, TSMC, Qualcomm, NVIDIA, Micron); re-qual is 12–18 months. Consumable razor/blade model. >3.5% R&D (TSV probe cards, Tri-Temperature probes, 100GHz sockets).

**Ownership — GOVERNANCE FLAG:** Founder Lee Chae-yoon (b.1950) ~34.66% pre-sale; in Apr–May 2026 announced sale of ~7m shares (~9.18%) for ~₩863B (~$591M), cutting stake to ~25.5%. Children reportedly unwilling to take over; live succession risk (could trigger an M&A premium OR a prolonged overhang). Institutions ~43%.

**Economics:** FY2025 TTM revenue ~₩263bn (~$263M, +~30%). Operating margin ~37% historically; Q3 2025 cited at 49.8% (AI/HBM mix). Net margin ~33% TTM. ROIC ~15% (single source). Net cash ₩68.3bn. Trailing P/E ~279x (distorted); fwd P/E ~37–44x; P/B ~55x (asset-light); div yield ~0.6%. Stock ~₩110,800 (52-wk ₩39,050–₩129,000; +~170% from trough).

**Valuation:** 37–44x fwd on a ~40%+ margin monopoly consumable with net cash — not cheap conventionally, but a Western analogue would fetch 40–60x; Korea discount = fairly-to-modestly-cheap.

**10-yr bull case:** AI/HBM complexity flywheel; #1 supplier in 1,000+ fabs with near-zero switching. If revenue compounds 15–20% for 5 yrs at 40–45% margins, earnings ~₩400–500bn by 2030; 30x → 3–5x. Secondary: M&A premium given unclear succession.

**Risks:** (1) Succession/governance (live now). (2) Korean memory-downcycle concentration (Samsung/SK Hynix). (3) Long-term contactless-test substitution.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Bioventix** — comparable margin quality on a larger profit base, harder structural tailwind, more liquidity, Korea/Asia semi exposure (geographic gap). **Conviction: 3.5/5** (business 5/5, governance/timing 2/5).

---

## GEM 2: HANSOL CHEMICAL
- **Ticker:** 014680.KS (KOSPI) | **Country:** South Korea | **Mkt cap:** ~₩3.41tn (~$2.47B) | **Stage:** Compounder transitioning from commodity chemicals to spec-in semiconductor materials.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Pivoted from bulk hydrogen peroxide to mission-critical ALD/CVD precursors for sub-3nm fabrication. Two waves: (1) ALD precursor chemistry as the new sub-3nm bottleneck (ppt purity; qualified at Samsung, SK Hynix, TSMC, reportedly Intel/Micron); electronic materials now ~45% of profit mix and growing; (2) geopolitical diversification away from Japanese suppliers (Stella Chemifa, Kanto, Sumitomo) post-2019. Under-covered: indexed as a "chemicals company," no ADR, minimal English IR, ~$2.5B cap, ~12 Korean analysts / ~zero Western.

**Moat:** Spec-in qualification (12–24 month approval into a fab's process module, zero defect tolerance) = among the highest switching costs in materials. 20+ years of ultra-high-purity H₂O₂ know-how (99.9999%+). Local-supply preference; co-development with Samsung/SK Hynix R&D.

**Ownership — flag:** Cho family (descendants of Samsung founder via Hansol Group) ~16.4% (Cho Dong-hyeok ~15.07% + related). NPS ~14% ("active investor"). Family reportedly seeking a "white knight" — control fragility; activism plausible (could force better returns OR distract management).

**Economics:** Revenue ₩769→885→772→776→884bn (FY21–25); op income ₩198→186→124→129→156bn; op margin 25.7%→16.1%→17.7% (trough then re-expanding); net income ₩148→155→105→123→147bn; FCF FY2025 ₩117bn (recovering after FY2023 −₩15bn precursor capex); EPS FY2025 ₩13,428. ROE ~14–21%; low leverage.

**Valuation:** Share ₩280,500 (52-wk ₩120,200–346,500); P/E ~22.5x; consensus target ₩374,000 (+33%). EV/EBITDA est 10–12x. Best risk/reward in the lane — discount reflects mis-classification + governance + the flat 2021–24 period (all potentially temporary). Japanese ALD comps 15–25x.

**10-yr bull case:** Electronic materials to 50–60% of revenue by 2030; margins back to 22–25%; FCF compounds 20%+; multiple re-rates to 30–35x as Western investors discover the semi-materials story = 3–5x. Adjacent options: silicon anode (EV batteries), QD display materials.

**Risks:** (1) Governance/family-control fragility + NPS activism. (2) H₂O₂/Samsung-foundry cyclicality (~30% of H₂O₂ revenue from Samsung). (3) Capacity-ramp execution (Korea + US lines; front-loaded capex cut Q4 2025 op profit).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Sartorius Stedim** — both spec-in consumable suppliers, but Hansol has a structural upturn, better visibility, 22x vs Sartorius's recovery 50–70x. Also fills Korean semi-materials gap. **Conviction: 4/5.**

## Taiwan runner-up
**Chroma ATE** — extraordinary (NVIDIA SLT gate for every GB200, 61–65% GM) but +633%/52wks, P/E 80–100x = "already discovered." Needs a 50%+ correction to be interesting.

## Verification caveats
Leeno Q3 op margin ~49.8% (single source); FY2025 revenue ~$263M (companiesmarketcap); ROIC figures from aggregators not cross-verified vs DART filings. Hansol electronic-materials ~45% of profit from simplywall.st narrative (not primary). ROIC for both flagged unverified.

# US Small/Mid-Cap Under-Covered Quality Compounders

_For a 15-stock concentrated quality-compounder book; light on US/USD; seeking founder-aligned picks-and-shovels into structural waves._

## Executive Summary
Four names clear the bar to displace an incumbent. Comfort Systems (FIX) disqualified — at ~$35B it is no longer under-covered. The four gems:
1. **OTC Markets Group (OTCM)** — monopoly financial-infrastructure tollgate
2. **InterDigital (IDCC)** — recurring patent-royalty machine into 5G/6G/AI video
3. **Installed Building Products (IBP)** — founder-led insulation installer into AI data centers + housing
4. **Kinsale Capital (KNSL)** — founder-led technology-native E&S insurer

---

## Gem 1: OTC MARKETS GROUP (OTCM)
- US | OTC Pink | ~$640M | Mature compounder entering a new growth phase.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Operates the infrastructure beneath the entire non-exchange US securities ecosystem — 12,000+ securities, $445B+ annual dollar volume, three tiers (OTCQX, OTCQB, Pink). Waves: (1) global capital-market democratization (foreign ADRs without full SEC registration = 75% of securities, 85% of volume); (2) tokenization/digital-asset securities (federal approval to trade on OTC Link); (3) 24-hour/overnight trading (MOON ATS +52% revenue Q1 2026). Under-covered: trades OTC (most desks structurally can't own it), ~$640M below most mandates, no sell-side, CEO doesn't court Wall Street.

**Moat:** Tollbridge/network-effect monopoly — any broker quoting an OTC security must use OTC Link ATS; no competing platform at scale/standing. Corporate-services listing fees (recurring, switching costs); market-data licensing (pure software margin). 131 employees, ~$48M FCF on $125M revenue (38%+ FCF margin).

**Ownership:** Cromwell Coulson (CEO since acquiring the predecessor in 1997) ~27–34% (sources vary; 27.7% personal + 7.4% family trust per one; 34.48% direct per another). Insiders/officers/directors >34%.

**Economics (2025):** Gross revenue $125.3M (+13%); operating income $38.2M (30.5%); net income $31.1M (+14%); adj EBITDA $47.6M (+15%); FCF ~$48.4M (~38% margin); 13-yr revenue CAGR ~12%; ROIC 47–58% (5-yr avg ~58%); zero debt; Q1 2026 revenue $34.8M (+14%).

**Valuation:** ~$53–55 share; ~$640M cap; P/E ~20x (14% below 10-yr median 23x); FCF yield ~7.5%; consensus target $65 (+18%). For 58% avg ROIC, zero debt, 12% CAGR, structural monopoly — anomalously cheap.

**10-yr bull:** Preferred on-ramp for tokenized securities/digital assets; MOON ATS captures 24-hr trading share; ADR/foreign-listing volumes expand. To 28–30x on $80–90M net income → $2.4–2.7B cap = 3.5–4x + dividends. Coulson likely never sells.

**Risks:** (1) Regulatory restructuring of OTC rules / competing ATSs (existential moat risk; but adapted in 2014 Pink cleanup). (2) Growth ceiling — core has limited pricing power; new products unproven at scale (one source projects only 5% 5-yr CAGR).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** — structural US financial-infrastructure alpha, founder-operator 30%+, near-zero cyclicality, idiosyncratic upside. **Conviction: 5/5** — closest thing to a hidden gem in US markets today.

---

## Gem 2: INTERDIGITAL (IDCC)
- US | NASDAQ | ~$6.5B | Established compounder, re-rating phase.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Invents/licenses foundational wireless + video-compression tech — every 3G/4G/5G device and modern video codec potentially owes a royalty. Waves: 5G (80% of global smartphones licensed; ARR +24% to $582M); AI video (Deep Render acquisition for AI-native codecs); 6G (world's-first 6G sensing architecture with Türk Telekom at MWC 2026); IoT/fintech licensing. Under-covered: misperceived as a "patent troll"; unusual model (near-zero COGS, high litigation overhead, lumpy settlements); ~26M shares, heavy institutional ownership, low retail visibility.

**Moat:** ~38,000-patent portfolio (7 new/day, doubled 2017–2025) embedded in global wireless/video standards — legally mandated infrastructure. FRAND licensing = court-backed recurring revenue. Won vs Apple, Huawei, ZTE. ARR $582M (+24%) = contracted future cash.

**Ownership:** Not founder-led (CEO Liren Chen since 2023); insiders ~1.9% (weakness), but aggressive buybacks ($169M in 2025; share count >50M→~26M over a decade; >$1B authorized).

**Economics (2025):** Revenue $834M (−4% YoY, 2024 was a record; 3-yr CAGR ~6%); ARR $582M (+24%); adj EBITDA $589M (71% margin, record); net income $407M (+13%, record); FCF $474M (~57% margin); ROIC 25–30%; net cash ~$780M; operating margin 49.7%.

**Valuation:** ~$6.5–8.4B cap; EV/EBIT ~14x; EV/EBITDA ~12x; P/FCF ~11x; FCF yield ~8.9%; 9.5% short interest (squeeze fuel). Materially cheap for a 57%-FCF-margin monopoly-adjacent IP engine.

**10-yr bull:** ARR to $1B+ by 2030 (mgmt target); AI-video licensing +$200–300M ARR by 2030; 6G cycle ~2030–2032; with ~25M shares and $1B+ net income by 2032 → $80–120/share at 15x = 2–3x.

**Risks:** (1) Court/arbitration ruling against FRAND rates (China most sensitive). (2) 6G standardization delay to 2035+ compresses the case. (Plus low insider alignment.)

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **VinaCapital Vietnam** — asset-light recurring IP royalty engine vs pure EM macro; fills US/USD gap. **Conviction: 4/5** (docked for low insider ownership + lumpy litigation).

---

## Gem 3: INSTALLED BUILDING PRODUCTS (IBP)
- US | NYSE | ~$5.6B | Quality consolidator, mid-cycle.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Installs insulation (+ complementary products) across residential, commercial, and — increasingly — data centers. Waves: US housing shortage (installs 25%+ of all new US residential insulation); tightening energy codes; AI data-center buildout ($710B+ NA infra spend 2026, specialized thermal insulation); renovation/retrofit of aging stock. Under-covered: classified as "homebuilder services," seen as cyclically peaking; data-center/commercial opportunity under-appreciated; ~$5.6B too small for big funds.

**Moat:** Hyperlocal, labor-intensive install — can't ship across state lines; needs local crews/relationships/trucks. 250+ branches, 10,000+ employees = scale in local labor, insurance, training, builder relationships. Builders value scheduling reliability. Efficient scale (3–4 regional players + an independent; a fifth destroys economics). ROIC ex-goodwill ~41%; FCF conversion ~90%+.

**Ownership:** Jeff Edwards (CEO/Chairman) owns 14.4% (3.9M shares, Dec 2025), 30+ yrs in the industry, co-founder.

**Economics (2025):** Revenue ~$2.97–3.0B (record, +~1%, weather/demand headwinds H1); adj EBITDA $518.5M (17.5%); GAAP EBITDA $496M; net income $265M (diluted EPS $9.71); op CF $371M, capex ~$34M → FCF ~$340–370M (~5.6% yield); ROIC ex-goodwill ~41%; net leverage 1.1x (target <2x); cash $321.9M; insider 13.86%.

**Valuation:** ~$5.6B cap; P/E ~22x; EV/EBIT ~17x; EV/EBITDA ~13x; P/FCF ~18x. Not cheap absolutely but reasonable for quality + tailwinds; down from $300+ highs on housing-start concerns (entry window).

**10-yr bull:** Starts recover to 1.5M+/yr; commercial/data-center demand compounds separately; disciplined M&A (50+ deals/10 yrs) adds adjacent services. To $5–6B revenue by 2034 at maintained margins, 20x earnings → $10–12B cap = ~2x + dividends.

**Risks:** (1) Prolonged housing downcycle (~70% residential). (2) Labor-cost inflation compressing margins.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Veralto** — comparable picks-and-shovels quality at lower multiple, 14% founder ownership, 41% ROIC, data-center tailwind, fills US gap. **Conviction: 4/5.**

---

## Gem 4: KINSALE CAPITAL (KNSL)
- US | NYSE | ~$7.0B | Quality compounder, growth normalizing.

**Structural wave & under-coverage:** Purest play on Excess & Surplus (E&S) lines — risks too unusual/novel for standard admitted carriers. E&S grew ~$75B (2022) → $100B+ (2024) on climate, cyber, construction liability, supply-chain disruption. E&S carriers price freely and customize terms — structurally superior for a disciplined underwriter. Secular, irreversible migration as the world gets riskier (AI liability, weather). Under-covered: niche/obscure; insurance under-owned by quality-growth investors; $7B in small/large no-man's-land.

**Moat:** Technology-driven underwriting discipline — proprietary in-house IT platform (faster broker quotes, ~20.8% expense ratio vs industry ~32%); single business segment (refuses to diversify into easier admitted lines); combined ratio 71.7% Q4 2025 (industry ~95–100%); AI in underwriting/claims; broker loyalty from fast reliable quotes.

**Ownership:** Michael Kehoe (founder/CEO, founded 2009) ~9.15% (2.13M shares, ~$950M); deeply aligned.

**Economics (2025):** GWP ~$2.0B; net income (9M) $365M (FY implied ~$480–500M); operating ROE 26%; combined ratio FY avg ~76% (Q4 71.7%); book value/share $84.66 (+33%); op CF ~$1.0B; ROIC 21.4%; float $3.1B (rising-rate beneficiary); insider 5.39% (Kehoe ~9.15%).

**Valuation:** ~$7.0B cap; P/E ~13.4x; EV/EBIT ~10.5x; P/FCF ~6.9x; FCF yield ~14.4%. Strikingly cheap for a 76% combined ratio, 26% ROE, founder-led name. Pulled back 40%+ from $512 high to ~$305 on E&S-growth deceleration (30%+→low-double-digits) — real, but the business remains exceptional.

**10-yr bull:** E&S market $100B→$200B+ by 2035; Kinsale holds ~2% share while expanding into cyber/AI/climate; 26% ROE compounds book 20%+/yr → BVPS $600–700 by 2034; at 2x book → $14–15B = ~2x + dividends.

**Risks:** (1) E&S deceleration as admitted carriers reclaim share in a soft cycle; cat losses / AI-liability tort cycle could spike combined ratio to 95%+. (2) Re-rating risk — peaked at 40–50x earnings; multiple may compress before growth re-accelerates.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Secunet** — adds US/USD, different wave (E&S insurance), 9% founder stake, FCF yield 14% vs Secunet's lower; near recession-resistant. **Conviction: 4/5** (edges past ideal cap; growth deceleration to monitor; insurance is binary-risk).

## Rejected
Comfort Systems/FIX (~$35B, discovered); Cactus/WHD (oil-services cyclicality, 54x on depressed earnings, 1% insider); McGrath/MGRC (no founder, slow); Clearfield/CLFD (too small, GAAP loss, no moat vs Corning/CommScope); NV5/NVEE (roll-up, thin margins, goodwill); Haynes/HAYN (commodity inputs); Frontdoor/FTDR (no founder, churn challenges).

# AI / Data-Center Compute Buildout — Picks & Shovels

**Lane: power delivery · liquid cooling · advanced packaging · precision interconnect. NOT Nvidia/Broadcom/TSMC/SMCI.**

## Summary
| Rank | Name | Ticker | Country | Mkt cap | Conviction |
|------|------|--------|---------|---------|-----------|
| 1 | Vicor | VICR | US | ~$15B | 4/5 |
| 2 | nVent Electric | NVT | Ireland/US | ~$18B EV | 4/5 |
| 3 | BE Semiconductor | BESI | Netherlands | ~€22B | 3/5 |
| 4 | Fabrinet | FN | Cayman/Thailand | ~$23B | 3/5 |

Excluded: **Modine (MOD)** — great story but FCF currently negative (−$20M LTM), ~120x P/E TTM, no founder, +179%/52wks (thesis real, entry not). **Aalberts (AALB)** — data center only ~2% of revenue, organic −2.5%.

---

## GEM 1: VICOR (VICR)
- US (Andover, MA) | ~$15.3B | Mid-cap, profitable, re-accelerating.

**Wave & under-coverage:** AI compute has a physics problem — today's GPU racks draw 200–400kW; next-gen Rubin/GB300 racks exceed 1,000kW. Moving 12V→48V cuts I²R losses 16x, and Vicor's Factorized Power Architecture (FPA) / Vertical Power Delivery (VPD) modules go 48V→sub-1V at the package in a 1.5mm slice. Under-covered: lost Nvidia H100 to Monolithic Power (2023) and was written off; founder-controlled, no Wall Street IR; revenue flat-to-down 2022–24 (trough). Re-discovered after Q1 2026 (+20% revenue, backlog $301M +70% sequential, book-to-bill >2.0).

**Moat:** (1) Proprietary CHiP fab process — can't run on standard CMOS lines; own Andover fab ($1B→$1.5B capacity). (2) Patent/IP — second ITC case vs Delta targeting the VPD topology required for Nvidia Rubin; FY2026 guidance excludes new licensing income pending the 2027 ITC determination. (3) 2nd-gen VPD spec gap — 3 A/mm², 40x current multiplication, 1.5mm thickness; no competitor has matched at 2,000A+ for Rubin/B300.

**Ownership:** Dr. Patrizio Vinciarelli (founder/Chair/CEO) ~19.3%; supervoting control. Active selling at $334 = diversification (still holds $3B+) — monitor velocity.

**Economics:** FY2025 revenue $407.7M; FY2026 guidance ~$570M; FY2025 GM 57.3%, op margin ~18%, net margin 26.2%; Q1 2026 $113M (+20%); backlog $301M; net cash; ~15% ROA. 9M-to-Sep-2025 op cash flow $123.8M, capex ~$4M/qtr.

**Valuation:** P/E ~83x TTM / ~47x fwd; ~$15B for $570M revenue — prices a licensing win + Rubin design-win.

**10-yr bull:** By 2030 VPD = standard power layer in every rack >500kW; revenue $2–3B (product + licensing $200–500M/yr if ITC succeeds); 30x EBIT on $600M+ EBIT → $18–20B from licensing alone.

**Risks:** (1) Design-win risk on Rubin/B300 (no confirmed wins disclosed). (2) ITC outcomes. (3) Valuation (was $40 in early 2024).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** — the "early Micron/AMD" pattern (written off, re-emerging as indispensable). **Conviction: 4/5.**

---

## GEM 2: nVENT ELECTRIC (NVT)
- Ireland-domiciled, NYSE | ~$18B EV / ~$14–15B cap | Accelerating growth.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Every AI rack needs liquid cooling = leak-proof, pressure-rated, isolated manifolds, CDUs, enclosures. nVent (SCHROFF, HOFFMAN, Eldon) is one of ~3 global firms with the scale/certifications/hyperscaler qualification at gigawatt scale. Under-covered: spun from Pentair 2018 as a "boring enclosures" business — the AI reinvention hasn't caught up with institutional memory; Cramer called it "mini Vertiv" Mar 2026. Data-center revenue ~$1B 2025 (~26% of sales → guided >55% in Q1 2026). Ecolab/CoolIT deal at 29x NTM EBITDA (Mar 2026) is an independent comp.

**Moat:** Hyperscaler-qualification lock-in (sole-sourced per generation; re-qual on live clusters impractical); CoolIT partnership + >1GW deployed since 2020 (reference density Eaton/Legrand can't match quickly); US re-shoring leverage (new Minnesota plant 2026); $2.6B backlog (Q1 2026) = 8–12 months; organic orders +40% Q1 2026.

**Ownership:** No founder (CEO Beth Wozniak); institutional. Capital discipline offsets (FCF conversion 90–95% guided; ROIC ex-GW/intangibles 38.4%).

**Economics:** FY2025 revenue $3.89B (+29.5%); adj EPS $3.35 (+35%); FCF ~$561M; Q4 op margin 19.7%; FY2026 guidance +26–28% reported / +21–23% organic; Q1 2026 $1.242B (+53% reported, +34% organic), backlog $2.6B; adj EPS 2026 $4.00–4.15; ROIC ex-GW 38.4% (reported 9.45% — goodwill drag); EV/EBITDA 24.5x; EV/FCF ~55x; ND/EBITDA 1.6x.

**10-yr bull:** Dominant non-captive liquid-cooling supplier; revenue $4B→$8B+ by 2030; margins to 22–25%; FCF/share +20%; 20x EBIT on $1.6–2.0B EBIT → $32–40B EV vs $18B. Acquisition optionality at the 29x CoolIT comp.

**Risks:** (1) Immersion cooling superseding DLC. (2) Hyperscaler vertical integration. (3) M&A integration/goodwill.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **VinaCapital Vietnam** — fills the electrical/mechanical infrastructure layer of the AI supercycle. **Conviction: 4/5** (no founder; size caps upside vs Vicor).

---

## GEM 3: BE SEMICONDUCTOR (BESI)
- Netherlands | ~€22.5B (~$24B) | Cycle trough-to-recovery.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Every AI chip (HBM4/chiplets) needs hybrid bonding — sub-micron Cu-Cu interconnect replacing solder bumps (doubles bandwidth, halves power). BESI = acknowledged global leader; >150 cumulative system orders, 18 customers; AMAT took 9% stake Apr 2025. Under-covered: Dutch-listed, poor ADR liquidity, sits in "European industrial" bucket; ~15 analysts vs 40+ for ASML.

**Moat:** <10nm alignment accuracy (competitors Hanmi/ASMPT 12–18 months behind); Bernstein sees Samsung/SK Hynix/Micron "all highly likely" to adopt for HBM4; AMAT co-development (full stack); Investor-Day target €1.5–1.9B revenue at 40–55% op margins (vs €173M op income 2025); Lam/AMAT takeover interest = downside floor (~€270–300/share).

**Adversarial:** Already crowded. €284/share = P/E ~151x TTM / ~75x fwd; EV/EBITDA ~96x; EV/Revenue ~36x. On €1.5–1.9B revenue at 40% margins → €680M EBIT at 30x = €20B EV ≈ today. 10-yr bull is priced in unless margins exceed targets.

**Ownership:** Richard Blickman (founder/CEO since 1995) ~1.7%; AMAT 9%; institutions 64%; 95% payout (cuts both ways).

**Economics (FY2025):** Revenue €591.3M (−2.7%); GM 63.3%; EBIT margin 29.3%; net margin 22.3%; net income €131.6M; op cash flow €178.1M; net cash €36M; Q1 2026 revenue €184.9M (+28.3%), orders €269.7M (+104.5%).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Bachem** — adds advanced-packaging exposure, but more cyclical; price matters. **Conviction: 3/5** — buy-on-dips (4/5 at €180–200; balanced at €284).

---

## GEM 4: FABRINET (FN)
- NYSE (Cayman/Thailand) | ~$23B | High-growth.

**Wave & under-coverage:** AI clusters need photonic interconnects — 400G/800G/1.6T transceivers to move data between GPUs. Fabrinet is the dominant contract manufacturer for optical transceivers (76% of revenue optical comms; DCI ~doubled YoY Q1 FY2026). Misframed as a low-margin "contract manufacturer" — actually a precision micro-assembly monopolist at micron tolerances with 25-yr sole-source ties (Coherent, Ciena, Nvidia).

**Moat:** 99.7% yields at micron tolerances (100–1000x tighter than PCB); re-qualifying an optical partner takes 18–24 months; sole-source on 1.6T modules (JPM sees $1.5B market 2026, Fabrinet majority); asset-light, $0 net debt, $969M cash; new AWS partnership diversifies beyond Nvidia/Cisco.

**Adversarial:** Nvidia ~30% + Cisco ~18% = ~48% from two customers — central bear case. Counter: optical manufacturing more specialized than what Nvidia has in-sourced; Building 10 expansion suggests Nvidia commitment.

**Ownership:** Founder Tom Mitchell retired Oct 2025 (25 yrs); CEO Seamus Grady professional; low insider.

**Economics:** Q1 FY2026 revenue $978M (+22%); FY2025 ~$3.6B (+19%, record); GM ~22.1%; op margin ~18.2%; net margin ~9.8–9.9%; FCF ~$206M; cash $969M; debt $0; ROE 18.8%; Q2 guidance $1.05–1.1B; P/E ~55x TTM / ~41x fwd; EV/EBITDA ~47x.

**10-yr bull:** Every AI DC for Rubin successors needs 1.6T–3.2T interconnects at 10–20x density; Building 10 doubles capacity; revenue $6–8B by 2030; op margins to 12–14%; FCF $700M–$1B; 30x FCF → $21–30B.

**Risks:** (1) Customer concentration/in-sourcing (catastrophic). (2) Thailand single-country FX/tariff (baht −11.8% since 2023). (3) Silicon-photonics co-packaging displacement (5–10 yr).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** or **Vietnam** — precision-manufacturing moat in the data-transmission layer; Thailand base adds indirect EM-Asia exposure with real quality. **Conviction: 3/5** (customer concentration caps it at 4/5).

## Recommended two-name add
**VICR + NVT** — orthogonal layers (chip-level power; rack-level cooling), low correlation to each other and the book, fill the blind spot comprehensively.

## "AI capex pause" resilience
NVT best protected ($2.6B backlog, embedded cooling) > VICR (backlog + non-capex licensing) > FN (sole-source re-qual cost, but 51% in two customers) > BESI most exposed (equipment orders deferred first; 2024 showed −2.7%).

## GEM 1: POWELL INDUSTRIES (POWL)
- US (Houston) | Nasdaq | ~$3.2B (down from ~$6B peak early 2026) | Mid-cap, inflection underway.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Custom-engineered medium-voltage switchgear, switchboards, power-control for utilities, oil & gas, data centers, LNG, industrial. Pure play on grid modernization + industrial electrification + AI data-center power. Nearly tripled revenue in 4 yrs, ROIC ~33%, yet only 8 analysts — a small-cap profile for a $3B company sitting in a classification blind spot.

**Moat:** Fully custom-engineered per order (specialty engineering + recurring hardware); mission-critical placement (2–3 yr qualification); dominant NA maker of arc-flash/explosion-proof switchgear for petrochem/LNG (3–6 month lead-time premiums); service flywheel; backlog $1.8B (Mar 2026) vs $1.1B LTM revenue = 18 months; electric-utility segment +50% in FY2025.

**Ownership:** Founded 1947 by William Powell; son Thomas Powell long-time CEO, ~17% (post-2025 unverified). Institutions ~52%.

**Economics (FY ends Sep):** Revenue FY21–25: $471→533→699→1,012→1,104M; net income $1→14→55→150→181M; gross margin 16%→29%; EBITDA $11→225M; EPS $0.02→$4.95. 4-yr CAGR ~24%; ROIC 33–35%; net cash $476M (zero debt, >15% of cap); FCF ~$155M FY2025 (~14% yield); backlog $1.8B (3x from $600M FY2022).

**Valuation:** ~$3.2B (−40% from peak). P/E ~17–18x; EV/EBITDA ~12–13x (discount to Eaton 34x, ABB, Schneider); P/FCF ~20x. Defensible with $1.8B backlog floor.

**10-yr bull:** Grid supercycle 2024–2035+; AI DCs need purpose-built switchgear; 6+ NA LNG terminals need explosion-proof gear; Remsdaq acquisition extends services. 15–20%/yr for 3–4 yrs then 8–10%; revenue $2.5–3B, EPS $10–15 → stock $300–450 vs ~$250.

**Risks:** (1) Margin cyclicality (low-teens pre-2022; could revert 800–1000bps if ABB/Eaton flood custom segment). (2) Order lumpiness/concentration (LNG/utility).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** — US/NA real-economy infrastructure absent from the book. **Conviction: 4/5.**

- Spain (Basque) | BME Main Market since Feb 2026 | ~€1.83B (+~339%/1yr) | Small-mid cap, just re-rated.

Every new substation/HVDC converter/offshore-wind collector needs them. Virtually unknown outside Spain until early 2025; only moved to Main Market Feb 2026 (institutional discovery just beginning).

_Genuine compounders, not cyclical energy plays. Fills the book's single biggest blind spot: zero energy/real assets._

## PICK 1: TOPAZ ENERGY (TPZ.TO)
- Canada | TSX | ~C$5.0B (~US$3.7B) | Royalty/infrastructure hybrid, growth phase.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Sits at WCSB gas development for LNG Canada + the Clearwater oil play. Tourmaline (Canada's largest gas producer) tripling LNG-linked volumes 213,000→333,000 mmbtu/d by exit-2028 on lands where Topaz holds royalty + infrastructure interests. Infrastructure piece invisible to E&P/midstream screeners. ~17 analysts, no US index inclusion.

**Moat:** Royalty stream (~75% of revenue) — no capex/opex, 17.5% on fee lands, no abandonment liability. Infrastructure (~25%) — owns processing/water-handling that Tourmaline/Tamarack/Headwater are contractually obligated to run through (min-volume commitments; 92–96% op margins). ~90% of royalty lands operated by 3 disciplined operators. Acreage geologically defined, permanent, irreplaceable.

**Ownership:** Tourmaline ~15% (down from 21%, selling — modest flag, but it's the anchor operator); total insider/strategic ~20%; management buying in 2025.

**Economics:** FY2025 revenue C$336.5M, EBITDA C$338.7M, FCF C$307.5M (91% margin); Q1 2026 revenue C$94.6M, FCF C$78.7M; 2026 guidance ~C$380M revenue; royalty production ~23,500–23,900 boe/d; dividend C$1.40 (66% payout); ND/EBITDA 1.5x (→<1.2x exit). Share C$32.51; EV ~C$5.5B; EV/EBITDA ~16x; P/FCF ~16x; yield ~4.3%. Dividends sustainable to US$55 WTI / ~zero AECO (verified). 16x vs Franco-Nevada 35–40x.

**10-yr bull:** LNG Canada Phase 2 + Asian demand (Tourmaline TTF-linked to 333,000 mmbtu/d by 2028); Clearwater (production +50% in 2025, decade runway); 66% payout leaves ~35% FCF to buy new royalties (Franco-Nevada-style flywheel; ~23% TSR/yr since 2020 IPO); infra tariffs CPI-linked. At 6% boe growth, $70 WTI/$3 AECO → FCF ~C$500M by 2033; 15x P/FCF → ~C$7.5B vs C$5B = ~10–12%/yr incl reinvested dividends.

**Risks:** (1) AECO gas pricing (70% gas; structurally weak Canadian gas). (2) Tourmaline concentration. (3) Tourmaline ownership decline.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **VinaCapital Vietnam** (or India ETF) — passive EM → genuine moat + capital-light royalty + inflation linkage + 4%+ growing yield; fills the energy/real-asset blind spot. **Conviction: 4/5.** If only one energy slot, Topaz wins (lower valuation, LNG catalyst, infra stabiliser, higher yield).

Pure fee-based midstream, 95% MVC through 2026 (→80% by 2028), ~$1.25B EBITDA at 6.92x, Chevron 37.8% anchor, ~8% yield. BUT single-basin (Bakken) concentration; Chevron both anchor and overhang (prefers 100% or exit); ~$200–250M capex/yr (~20% of EBITDA, not capital-light); 18+ analysts (not hidden); MLP-adjacent ownership friction. Income vehicle, not a compounder. **2/5.**

## Final
Add **Topaz** (replace VOF) — purest capital-light Canadian royalty+infra compounder, LNG Canada tailwind, 16x vs 35x peers. If two slots, add **PrairieSky** (replace India ETF) for the oil/acreage pairing.

# Critical Minerals & Mining Picks-and-Shovels

_Royalty/streaming, processors, low-cost long-life producers essential to electrification/defense/semis._

| Rank | Name | Ticker | Country | Mkt cap | Conviction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Aurubis | NDA.DE | Germany | ~€9.3B | 4/5 |
| 2 | Lynas | LYC.AX | Australia | ~A$19.3B | 3/5 |
| 3 | Uranium Royalty (post-Sweetwater) | UROY | Canada/US | ~$520M→EV~$2.5–3B | 3/5 |
| 4 | Ecora Royalties | ECOR.L | UK | ~£100–150M | 2/5 |

---

## CANDIDATE 1: AURUBIS (NDA.DE)
- Germany | Frankfurt / OTC AIAGF | ~€9.3B (~$10.2B) | Mature, capex inflection.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Simultaneously the world's largest copper recycler and Europe's indispensable copper smelter (~1.2Mt cathode-equivalent/yr from concentrates + complex scrap). Electrification lifts both concentrate and scrap feedstock; the EU Critical Raw Materials Act forces European manufacturers to source domestically recycled copper — which only Aurubis can fill at scale. Under-covered: German mid-cap, 10x earnings, seen as a "toll smelter" not a tech-enabled recycler; Richmond VA greenfield (first new US copper smelter in 40+ yrs) not yet in consensus.

**Moat:** Recovers 20+ metals from a single input (copper, gold, silver, PGMs, selenium, tellurium, bismuth) → implicit "negative cost" on some inputs as by-product credits exceed smelting cost; no Asian competitor can replicate without decades of metallurgy + the permit footprint. Network effects in European scrap sourcing (multi-year contracts); EU state-aid-cleared Complex Recycling Hamburg expansion = decade-long permitting barrier; embedded precious-metals call option; TC/RC hedge (recycling compensates when concentrate TCs tighten).

**Ownership:** Salzgitter AG ~22–25% (post bond conversion, unverified); free-float institutions ~39% (BlackRock, Vanguard); individuals ~25%. (Curiosity: Salzgitter's Aurubis stake reportedly worth more than Salzgitter's whole market cap.)

**Economics (FY2024/25):** Revenue €18.2B; operating EBT €355M; operating EBITDA €589M; FY2025/26 EBT guidance (upgraded May 2026) €425–525M; operating ROCE 8.1% (depressed by capex; target 10–12%); P/E ~10x; EV/EBITDA ~8.4x; div yield ~0.78%; incremental EBITDA ~€260M/yr from new projects from FY2028/29.

**Valuation:** ~€213/share = 10x earnings, 8.4x EV/EBITDA (10-yr median P/E ~9.5x). Market ignores ~€260M/yr incremental EBITDA (worth €1.5–2.5B NPV on current multiples), copper-price upside, recycled-content mandates, and the precious-metals option.

**10-yr bull:** Copper structurally to $5–6/lb; Richmond captures US onshoring premium; EU scrap mandates = captive regulated moat; +€260M EBITDA pushes EBT to €700–900M; re-rate 10x→14x → ~3x.

**Risks:** (1) Copper-price collapse in recession ($3/lb). (2) Richmond execution (limited track record; US trade-policy volatility). (3) Cyberattack/operational disruption (2022 precedent).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace one holdco (**EXOR** or **Investor AB**) — fills the real-asset/commodity-infrastructure blind spot with a quality toll-road on European copper flows and pricing power. **Conviction: 4/5.**

---

## CANDIDATE 2: LYNAS RARE EARTHS (LYC.AX)
- Australia (ops Malaysia/Australia) | ASX | ~A$19.3B (~US$12.5B) | Growth/scale-up.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Only scaled Western-aligned NdPr (EV-motor/wind/defense magnet material) processor outside China (~90% Chinese processing). China's Apr-2025 rare-earth export restrictions made Lynas a critical national-security asset for multiple Western governments. ASX-listed, no US ADR critical mass; pigeonholed as a volatile commodity stock.

**Moat:** Only scaled Western NdPr producer (LAMP Kuantan processes Mount Weld concentrate); 15+ yrs metallurgical IP; Mount Weld world's highest-grade deposit (20+ yr life); US DoD $258M for Seadrift TX heavy-RE separation; Malaysia renewed LAMP licence 10 yrs (to 2036); DoD offtake floor A$110/kg NdPr.

**Ownership:** CEO Amanda Lacaze (rescued from near-bankruptcy 2015), aligned non-founder; institutional (Australian super funds + global resource funds); effectively co-owned by Western governments via soft governance.

**Economics:** Revenue FY2025 A$556.5M (+20%); TTM A$715.9M (+48%); EBITDA A$101M (18% margin); net profit A$8m (near-zero, heavy reinvestment); NdPr 6,555t (+18%); price US$44–55/kg; cash A$166.5M; P/E ~224x TTM / ~39x fwd (FY2027E); ROIC ~0.25% (capex-depressed). A$750m raise Aug 2025 + A$1.5B+ capex (Kalgoorlie, Texas, Malaysia) suppress GAAP earnings.

**Valuation:** A$19/share, 39x fwd — not cheap; correct framing is optionality + throughput. NdPr at US$80–100/kg → EBITDA A$400–600M on same volumes (4–6x uplift, near-zero incremental cost).

**10-yr bull:** China restrictions persist; OEMs/defense pay a strategic premium; NdPr recovers to US$80–100/kg; Kalgoorlie becomes largest non-Chinese RE hub; Texas heavy-RE; revenue 3–4x; margins back to 40%+; re-rate 8x→15–20x EV/EBITDA = 5–10x.

**Risks:** (1) NdPr price crash (China floods market; entire bull case depends on elevated NdPr). (2) Texas Seadrift looks "unlikely to proceed" under current US politics. (3) Simultaneous-ramp execution complexity.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Bachem** or **Sartorius** — "indispensable process bottleneck" thesis in a more defensible geopolitical chain, but more commodity/price risk. **Conviction: 3/5.**

---

## CANDIDATE 3: URANIUM ROYALTY CORP / NEW URC (UROY)
- Canada-domiciled, US-listed | ~$520M pre-deal; post-deal EV ~$2.5–3B | Development-to-royalty inflection.

**Wave:** Nuclear renaissance (AI data centers, defense energy security, net-zero); uranium $85/lb (May 2026) vs $25 in 2020. Only pure-play uranium royalty on NASDAQ. **Moat:** first-mover monopoly in uranium royalties; capital-light (90%+ margins at scale); Apr-2026 Sweetwater combination (Orion + Ontario Teachers, ~$1.9B EV) transforms it into the largest US non-precious-metals royalty company.

**Economics:** Revenue TTM $40.25M; EBITDA $1.84M (pre-scale); cash $102M; negligible debt; Sweetwater EV ~$1.9B; physical uranium held. **Risks:** (1) Massive dilution — current holders → 41%; $813m new shares at $3.64 transfers most value to Orion/Teachers. (2) Sweetwater royalty quality uncertain (idle Wyoming mines). (3) Uranium mean-reversion to $60–70.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** as a uranium call option with royalty protection — but dilution/early-stage risk. **Conviction: 3/5.**

## CANDIDATE 4: ECORA ROYALTIES (ECOR.L)
- UK-listed | ~£100–150M | Producing royalty portfolio, scale-up.

Only LSE-listed royalty company repositioned around electrification minerals (copper ~50% of asset value, cobalt, nickel, uranium, RE). Post-restructuring (sold fossil-fuel royalties 2024–25, renamed from Anglo Pacific). **Moat:** diversified royalties (Mantos, Voisey's Bay, Caserones copper; Voisey's Bay cobalt stream; Denison uranium; Rainbow Rare Earths Phalaborwa — first royalty on a commercial RE-hydroxide producer outside China). Net debt ~$104M (−30% since Q1). **Economics:** H1 2025 portfolio contribution $17.9m (Kestrel coal timing-distorted; H2-weighted); base-metals +81% YoY; EBITDA guided 2x by 2027; ~50% discount to NAV (excl growth projects). **Risks:** Kestrel coal legacy (un-investable for ESG mandates); sub-£200M illiquidity; development-stage royalties (5–8 yr wait). **Conviction: 2/5** — better as a 2–3% starter than a full slot.

## Rejected
Yellow Cake (passive uranium fund, no moat); Iluka (cyclical trough, A$288m loss; Eneabba RE refinery exciting but 2027+ and net debt >A$1bn — revisit 12 months); MP Materials (~$14bn, light-RE heavy, fully covered); lithium (SQM/Albemarle/Ganfeng — structural oversupply, no moat vs price); Freeport/Glencore (large-cap, covered).

## Recommendation
Add **Aurubis** (replace EXOR or Investor AB) — Europe's indispensable copper processor at 10x with a 2028/29 EBITDA catalyst. Second: **Lynas** (replace India ETF) — specific high-moat NdPr vs passive basket. 2 in, 2 out; book stays at 15 with real critical-minerals exposure.

## GEM 1: RENK GROUP (R3NK.DE)
- Germany | Xetra | ~€5.7B | Post-IPO (Feb 2024), scaling.

**Wave & under-coverage:** The drivetrain of European land warfare — powerpack transmissions for the Leopard 2 (NATO's armored backbone), marine gearboxes for naval vessels, industrial slide bearings. Every new/upgraded Leopard 2 or frigate runs through RENK — the obscure mechanical heart inside the weapon system. Thinly covered post-IPO (Triton PE exited Aug 2025 at a 7x return); KNDS holds 15.8% (strategic anchor); analysts only now building coverage.

**Moat:** Military powerpacks need decades of per-platform qualification — no newcomer displaces a certified RENK transmission without multi-year NATO-wide re-qual; ~55% aftermarket/MRO (recurring, high-margin, grows with the fleet); dual moat (land + naval); €6.68bn backlog (Dec 2025) = 4.9x FY2025 revenue.

**Ownership:** KNDS N.V. 15.8% (needs RENK for its own products — won't sell); management (Rebecca Mgmt) 4.2%; Triton fully exited (overhang cleared); BlackRock ~3.5%, Norges ~2.1%.

**Economics (FY2025):** Revenue €1.37bn (+19.8%); adj EBIT €230m (16.9% margin; VMS segment 20.4%); backlog €6.68bn (record); book-to-bill 1.2x; FCF €67m (~47% conversion — low, capacity ramp); net debt €391m (1.5x). 2026 guidance: revenue >€1.5bn, adj EBIT €255–285m; 2030 target €2.8–3.2bn at >20% margin.

**Valuation:** EV ~€6.1bn; EV/EBITDA ~24x; P/E ~50x; P/S ~4.1x; EV/2026E EBIT ~22x. Not cheap, but 4.9x backlog = minimal revenue-visibility risk; off ~45% from €90 peak to ~€49 (some compression done).

**10-yr bull:** Zeitenwende structural; Bundeswehr to 203,000 soldiers + more Leopard 2s through 2032; Poland 1,000 tanks; Baltic naval build-out. At €3bn revenue / 20% EBIT (€600m) and 20x EV/EBIT → €12bn EV ≈ 2x current.

**Risks:** (1) Cycle reversal / political risk (backlog insulates several years). (2) Margin-target execution. (3) Valuation compression if rates rise (50x P/E).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** — German-defense growth optionality + a real moat + asset-specific upside; fills the absence of European industrial/hardware defense (Secunet is software). **Conviction: 4/5** — clearest blind spot; accumulate on weakness.

---

## GEM 2: EXAIL TECHNOLOGIES (EXA.PA)
- France | Euronext Paris (Compartment B) | ~€2.4B | Family-controlled, post-restructuring growth.

**Wave & under-coverage:** World leader in fiber-optic-gyroscope (FOG) inertial navigation systems and autonomous underwater vehicles — the navigational brains of submarines, surface vessels, torpedoes, autonomous mine-countermeasure drones, precision artillery. Navies protecting undersea cables / deploying autonomous mine-clearance need Exail. Invisible: was Groupe Gorgé until late 2022 (data aggregation broken); €2.4B Compartment-B cap; loss-making until 2025 (screened out by quality filters); ECA + iXblue combination only closed 2022.

**Moat:** iXblue = global #2 in FOG INS behind Northrop Litef, far more export-accessible — NATO navies wanting a non-US system (France, UK, Germany, India, Australia) come to Exail (sovereignty in its purest form); ECA AUVs dominate French Navy SLAMF; naval simulation; FOG photonic-assembly know-how built over decades.

**Ownership:** Gorgé family (Pelican Venture + Gorgé SA) 41.4% capital / 56.3% votes; Bpifrance minority; free float ~66% after Mar-2026 placement. **Flag:** Gorgé SA sold shares Mar 2026 — monitor disposal pace.

**Economics (FY2025):** Revenue €479m (+28%); "current EBITDA" €103m (+40%, ~21.5% margin, H2 23%); net income €6m (first profit; FY2024 −€5m); order intake €844m (+87%, record); backlog €1.1bn (+52%); net debt €114m (1.1x, excl ICG bonds); op cash flow >€100m; 2026+ guidance double-digit growth, 25% EBITDA target. EV ~€2.35bn; EV/2025 EBITDA ~23x; +~370% since early 2025.

**Flags:** "current EBITDA" is non-IFRS; GAAP net income depressed for years by iXblue amortization. ICG bonds excluded from stated net debt.

**10-yr bull:** FOG INS becomes sovereignty-critical for every NATO navy reducing US-GPS dependence; AUVs standard for EU mine-countermeasures. At €1bn revenue / 25% EBITDA (€250m) and 18x → €4.5bn EV vs €2.35bn = ~90% over 4 yrs.

**Risks:** (1) Family overhang/governance. (2) GAAP losses for years (index ineligibility). (3) French-Navy concentration (SLAMF).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **VinaCapital Vietnam** — family-controlled specialist (mirrors EXOR/Investor/Vitec/Topicus owner-operator pattern) with a deep naval-sovereignty moat. **Conviction: 3.5/5** (window may be closing post-+370%).

---

## GEM 3: CHEMRING GROUP (CHG.L)
- UK | LSE Main | ~£1.5B | Margin step-up.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Two segments — Countermeasures & Energetics (flares, decoys, chaff for aircraft/naval self-protection) and Sensors & Information (EW, CBRN detection, cyber). Largest air-countermeasures supplier in Europe; near-monopoly with UK/US/European air forces. Under-covered: history of operational problems (Tennessee, now resolved); "boring munitions"; ESG selling pressure. Countermeasures are consumables — fired, lost, replenished (recurring demand pool; Ukraine/Middle East de-stocking now being replenished).

**Moat:** UK's only qualified flare/decoy maker; US consolidated; consumable replenishment; CBRN/sensors growing; explosive-manufacturing facilities need 5–10 yr permitting/workforce/safety builds.

**Ownership — weakness:** No founder/family; insiders <1% (CEO 0.21%); institutional (Merian 7.9%, AXA 7.3%, Royal London 5.3%). Weakest ownership of the lane.

**Economics (FY2025, Oct YE):** Revenue £498m (+2%, +3% cc); underlying op margin 19.1% (+280bps); order book £1.3bn (+32%); underlying op cash £112.2m (114% of underlying EBITDA); net debt £89m. 2030 target ~£1bn. Mkt cap ~£1.5bn; P/E ~27x (sector avg 37–44x — cheapest in the lane). FCF currently negative (−£2.3m TTM; capex £93m capacity build; 3-yr conversion 101%).

**Adversarial:** Revenue only +2% despite the order book (capacity/operational lag — Tennessee now "delivering the plan" but still constraining); £1.3bn = 2.6x coverage (less than RENK's 4.9x), ~£413m earmarked for FY2026.

**10-yr bull:** Countermeasures resupply emergency; UK 2.5%→3% of GDP; Tennessee enables 20%+ US growth. At £1bn / 20% margins (£200m op) at 20x → £4bn = ~2.7x.

**Risks:** (1) Tennessee re-lapse (single biggest). (2) No owner-operator. (3) UK political/FX.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Bioventix** (marginal — Bioventix's near-monopoly + 50%+ margins make it the hardest to replace) or **Sartorius**. **Conviction: 3/5.**

---

## WATCH: VINCORION (V1NC.DE)
- Germany | Xetra | ~€800M | Fresh IPO (Mar 2026), post-PE exit (Star Capital).

Makes power generation/management/storage for the Leopard 2, Patriot, IRIS-T, Eurofighter — no weapon works without power. Platform-certified (re-qual the whole electrical architecture to switch); ~55% aftermarket. **Economics:** Revenue FY2025 €240m (+18%, +22% CAGR since 2022); EBIT €33.7m (+64%, 14.0% margin); net €19.4m; backlog €1.1bn total / €435m fixed (<2x revenue — thinner than it looks); Q1 2026 run-rate €284m (+40%); FY2026 EBIT margin guide 18–19%; IPO €17 (€850m cap). **Flags:** secondary-only IPO (PE exit, no growth capital); management stake undisclosed; no valuation margin of safety. **2.5/5 now → likely 4/5 in 12 months if execution proves out.**

## Also assessed (rejected)

## Recommendation
One name: **RENK** (replace India ETF) — clearest blind spot, exceptional visibility, de-rated entry. Two: add **Exail** (replace VOF) — family-controlled "early Vitec/Bachem" of defense electronics. Chemring = watch-list unless a dedicated UK-defense slot opens.

## GEM 1: FILTRONIC (FTC.L)
- UK | AIM | ~£858M (~$1.1B) | Small-cap, earnings ramping.

**Wave & under-coverage:** The LEO satellite revolution (Starlink v3+, Kuiper, OneWeb) needs tens of thousands of high-frequency mmWave transceivers, shifting from Ka-band (26 GHz) to E-band (71–86 GHz)/V-band. Filtronic's E-band GaN solid-state power amplifiers deliver >2x the output power of GaAs solutions at these frequencies. Invisible: AIM-listed; unimpressive 15-yr revenue history (~£15–25m pre-2024); no bulge-bracket coverage. £56m FY2025 revenue understates the £47.3m ($62.5m) SpaceX order (first deliveries FY2027).

**Moat:** Vertically integrated GaN design-to-manufacture (rare at this scale for mmWave space hardware); decade of E-band heritage; SpaceX de-risked it via a qualification ladder (£2m→£3.4m→£4.8m→£7.8m→£15.8m→£47.3m over 3 yrs); SpaceX holds warrants for up to ~15% (5% vested) — a co-dependency, not a customer; no obvious substitute (Qorvo/MACOM/Wolfspeed make chips, not space-qualified integrated subsystems); requalification of in-orbit units is prohibitive.

**Ownership:** Insiders ~43% (early 2025, likely incl founding management on restricted stock — precise breakdown unverified); SpaceX 5% (vested warrants) with board-observer-level interest.

**Economics:** FY2025 (May YE) revenue £56.3m (+121%); adj EBITDA £17.0m (30.2% margin); op profit £13.4m; cash £14.5m (May 2025) / £10.5m + £10m RCF (Nov 2025); H1 FY2026 revenue £25.3m (flat), op profit £2.6m (vs £6.8m — capacity investment phase), EBITDA £5.1m; ~90% of FY2026 covered by contract; net cash ex-leases £8.2m; P/E ~93x (FY2025, stock 390p); EV ~£851m. ROIC high but not reported (asset-light). FCF modest; op cash £3.4m H1 FY2026.

**Valuation:** P/E 93x elevated; relevant metric is fwd EV/revenue on the FY2027 ramp (£100m+ from SpaceX alone). FY2026 is a "valley" between legacy revenue and the ramp.

**10-yr bull:** $3bn LEO E-band hardware TAM (conservative if Kuiper/others also qualify); 5–10% share = $150–300m vs ~£56m; V-band, military satcom, 6G backhaul extend the runway; SpaceX warrants align both sides.

**Risks:** (1) Single-customer concentration (Americas 89.6% of H1 FY2026; SpaceX dominates; in-sourcing/MACOM risk — warrants mitigate not eliminate). (2) FY2026 earnings valley / delivery execution. (3) Already re-rated (3x in 12 months; 15.3x P/S prices a successful FY2027 ramp).

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **India ETF** — pure space-economy exposure (absent from the book); or complement E&Z. **Conviction: 4/5.**

---

## GEM 2: ASTRONICS (ATRO)
- US | NASDAQ | ~$3.1B | Multi-year recovery.

**Wave & under-coverage:** (1) 17,000+ aircraft order backlog globally, deliveries at a 7-yr high; (2) in-seat power spreading from premium to economy. Chronically under-followed (sits between "avionics" and "cabin interiors"; too small at $3.1B; 2022–24 supply-chain trough reputation).

**Moat:** EmPower in-seat power ~90% market share across 280+ airlines, 2M+ seats — seated under an STC, so replacement requires re-certification of every seat/aircraft type (certification moat + razor-blade aftermarket over 20–25 yr life); platform-qualified lighting (Airbus/Boeing/Embraer/Lockheed/Textron — 3–5 yr qualification locks supplier for program life); FAA ODA (via Envoy Aerospace 2025 acquisition — can self-certify STCs; very few small/mid-cap suppliers hold one); military avionics (ground-test, radio test, power distribution).

**Ownership:** Dual-class (Class B 10x votes); insider ~6.21% economic; Robert Keane (Cimpress CEO) ~20% of Class B, active chairman; CEO Peter Gundermann since 2014.

**Economics:** Revenue FY2025 ~$861.5M (vs ~$812M); Q1 2026 $230.6M (+12%); Q4 2025 op margin 14.8%; adj EBITDA Q1 2026 $37.9M (16.4%); FY2025 op CF $74.8M, capex $31.7M → FCF ~$43M; net debt ~$324M (Q3 2025); backlog $734.3M (record); FY2026 guidance $970M–$1B (raised); P/E ~70.6x; EV/EBIT 34.3x; EV/EBITDA 28.2x; P/FCF ~130x (depressed during ramp); ROIC ~19.2%; ROE ~21.2%.

**10-yr bull:** $1.5–2B revenue by 2032–34 (narrowbody in-seat-power retrofit, aftermarket leverage on 5,000+ equipped aircraft, ODA-enabled STC wins, military). At 18–20% EBITDA margins → FCF $150–200M on $2B → significant re-rating from 130x P/FCF.

**Risks:** (1) Boeing dependency (~11%; MAX/777X delays). (2) Leverage ($335M net debt + $225M 0%-convertible Sep 2025 = dilution). (3) Premium prices a smooth ramp.

**Portfolio fit:** Replace **Secunet** (smaller cap, limited runway) or **Sartorius** — US aerospace-aftermarket blind spot; TransDigm-like certification economics at a fraction of TransDigm's multiple. **Conviction: 4/5.**

## GEM 3: FREQUENCY ELECTRONICS (FEIM)
- US | NASDAQ | ~$747M | Micro/small-cap space heritage.

**Wave & under-coverage:** Precision timing/frequency generation — the backbone of every satellite, GPS, and military comms system. 1,000+ satellites since Voyager (1977). LEO constellations to 50,000+ by 2030 + DoD modernization (GPS IIIF, JADC2, quantum-referenced clocks). Only 1–2 analysts; 226 employees, $68m TTM revenue.

## GEM 4: CHEMRING (CHG.L)
_(Also in the defense lane.)_ ~£1.5B; NATO countermeasures sole-source (~85% NATO air, ~60% NATO naval), explosive-ordnance permitting moat, EW/threat-detection; record £1.345bn order book (76% of FY2026 contracted); FY2025 revenue £497.5m (+2%/+3% cc), underlying EBIT margin 14.8%, cash conversion 114%, net debt £89m (0.90x), ROIC ~12.2%; FCF −£2.3m TTM (capex £93m capacity build, recovers FY2027+); insider 0.41% (notable negative); EV/EBIT 21.8x, EV/EBITDA 16.3x, P/E 28.5x. **Conviction: 3/5** — genuine sole-source moats; penalised for near-zero insider ownership + negative current FCF. Replace **Sartorius** or **Secunet**.

## Rejected
Ducommun (negative FCF, internal-control weakness, ROIC 5.3%); FACC (~4.3% EBIT, AVIO control); Senior (ROIC ~3.9%); Porvair (good but 1% growth, no catalyst); Kaman (de-differentiated); HEICO/TransDigm (discovered, >30x EBIT).

## Strongest recommendation
**Filtronic** — SpaceX warrant alignment + differentiated GaN E-band + contracted FY2027 ramp; the "earnings valley" is the entry. Second: **Astronics** — proven certification moat (EmPower + FAA ODA), record revenue, 19% ROIC, FCF re-rating as working capital normalizes.
